5:## Context
11:## Locked decisions (from Saksham, this session)
14:2. **Capture**: continuous checkpointing via harness hooks **plus** an explicit `/handoff` finalize command that records reason-for-switching.
19:7. **Attribution (hard invariant)**: ZERO AI mentions in git history — no `Co-Authored-By`, no "Generated with Claude Code" — permanently. During initial construction (pre-v1), all commits are authored solely by `SakshamUboweja <ssakshamu@gmail.com>` with frequent, regular commits to main (contributions accrue to his GitHub profile); external human OSS contributors are welcome post-v1. Enforced via `{"attribution": {"commit": "", "pr": ""}}` in Claude Code settings (the old `includeCoAuthoredBy` boolean is deprecated) + a doctor check.
28:## Research findings that shape the design (verified 2026-07-11)
33:  - Claude Code: **`StopFailure` hook event** fires when a turn ends on an API error, with matcher filtering by error type (`rate_limit`, `overloaded`, `billing_error`, `server_error`, …) — the primary, structured limit-death signal. Output/exit code ignored (observability-only), so a checkpoint there is safe by construction. Text fallback: the stable strings `You've hit your session limit · resets …` / `weekly limit` / `Opus limit`.
34:  - Codex (>= 0.144): **lifecycle hooks exist** (`SessionStart`, `PreToolUse`, `PostToolUse`, `PreCompact`, `PostCompact`, `UserPromptSubmit`, `SubagentStop`, `Stop`; configured via `~/.codex/hooks.json`, `<repo>/.codex/hooks.json`, or config.toml `[hooks]`; stdin JSON with `session_id`, `cwd`, `model`, `transcript_path`). No StopFailure equivalent → limit death bounded by last Stop checkpoint. Text fallback: `You've hit your usage limit…`; `codex exec --json` exits non-zero.
40:## Prompting-guide distillation (for the AGENTS.md/CLAUDE.md templates)
46:## Design
50:### Core engine (`core/`)
54:**CLI surface** (`baton <cmd> --json`): `checkpoint`, `finalize`, `detect`, `remap`, `receive`, `init`, `doctor`, `status`, `purge-transcript`, `recover` (forced lock recovery). Frozen exit-code contract: `0` ok · `1` command failure · `2` usage error · `10` usage-limit · `11` throttle · `12` auth · `13` other-error. `--json` mode prints exactly one envelope `{ok, data, warnings, error}` on stdout. `baton wrap -- <cmd>` (PTY supervisor) is **deferred post-v1** — hook-driven detection suffices; the name/contract is reserved.
56:**Bundle** (`.handoff/`): single active bundle = `bundle.json` (atomic tmp+rename snapshot, `.bak` of previous good) + `journal.ndjson` (append-only events, torn-tail tolerant) + rendered `HANDOFF.md` + `history/` (unified retention: last 10 entries **per type** — finalize freezes, takeover archives, receive archives — each = frozen snapshot plus rotated journal; receipts needed for replay/audit are always preserved within retained entries). Load = snapshot + replay journal entries past `journalSeq`; recovery paths: `.bak` + replay, or full journal rebuild. Schema `baton/bundle@1`: origin platform/model, task goal/constraints/acceptance, plan steps with status, capped decisions log, files touched, role assignments, git branch/HEAD/dirty summary, handoff finalize block (`status: open|sealed|received` + `receive_log[]`), compaction markers. Size discipline is **deterministic truncation only** (decisions capped first-20+last-150 with marker; no LLM summarization).
58:**Concurrency** (Gate-1 additions, iterations 1+2): all bundle mutations run under a repo-scoped lock — `.handoff/lock` acquired via atomic `mkdir`/`O_EXCL`, containing `{writerId: <platform>-<pid>-<sessionHint>, fencingToken: <random per acquisition>, acquiredAt, heartbeatAt}`. **Lock model (iterations 3+4)**: mutation locks are **operation-scoped** — acquired for the duration of one command's mutation and released, never held across turns; logical session ownership lives in `bundle.json` (the active `origin`/generation), not in the lock. Lock metadata (host, pid, process start time, fencing token) is written atomically with acquisition; a crash between lock creation and metadata publication reads as torn metadata. Recovery rules, conservative on every OS: automatic recovery only when the owner is **provably dead** (same host, pid gone or pid/start-time mismatch); a same-host owner confirmed alive is **refused** — including `recover --force` — with the instruction to terminate that process first; missing/torn/cross-host/unverifiable owner metadata is treated as **unsupported for automatic recovery** (refusal with explanation), never as safe-to-steal. Fencing tokens remain a fast-abort layer. Tests: force attempted against a live paused writer (refused), crash between lock creation and metadata publication, owner paused after its final token check, torn-metadata refusal. Journal entries carry `writerId`; sequence numbers are allocated under the lock. **Semantic isolation**: v1 enforces one active task — a checkpoint whose `sessionHint`/origin doesn't match the active bundle is rejected with an actionable warning (`--take-over` supersedes: archives the current bundle to `history/` and starts fresh), so two unrelated sessions can't merge goals/files/decisions into one handoff. `receive` is a core-owned two-phase transaction. `receive --prepare` validates + resolves roles + returns a one-time receipt token and the resume prompt without mutating state; the token **binds** the bundle revision (journalSeq), normalized intake, config digest, target platform+session, the probe snapshot used for remap, **and a content-sensitive prepare-time git digest** — HEAD + branch + hashes of the staged and unstaged diffs + a bounded untracked-files digest (path + size + content hash, capped) — so a file that stays `M` but changes content still invalidates the token. `--commit` re-derives and compares every bound input, rejecting any drift (intervening checkpoint, config edit, HEAD move, content change, probe change) and requiring re-prepare; each declared drift input has its own rejection test. `receive --commit <token>` performs one atomic transition: `sealed → received` (normal) or `open → received` (the primary failure case: limit death before finalize; commit writes a receive-generated degraded seal recording intake origin/reason, flagged `degraded-seal`) — and in the same transition **adopts the receiving platform/session as the bundle's active owner and opens a fresh writable generation** (`generation` counter incremented, `status: open`), archiving the prior seal + receipt to `history/`. The receiving session's subsequent checkpoints are therefore first-party, and the bundle is immediately receivable again if the new platform also dies — chained A→B→C failover is contract-tested with checkpoints fired immediately after each receive. Cancellation or a competing receiver leaves state untouched; double-commit fails cleanly. Identical semantics on every adapter; six-direction contract tests cover sealed + unsealed variants. Tests: concurrent writers, provably-dead takeover, fencing-token abort incl. pause-after-final-check, foreign-session rejection/take-over, prepare-vs-checkpoint races, prepare-without-commit, double-commit, competing receivers, chained failovers.
60:**Journal rotation & crash recovery** (Gate-1 additions, iterations 1–3): on every finalize, crash-safe order = write a rotation marker → write frozen snapshot to `history/` → rename `journal.ndjson` alongside it → start a fresh journal seeded with the snapshot's `journalSeq` → clear marker. On load, a present marker deterministically rolls the rotation forward (if the snapshot landed) or back (if not); the same marker pattern governs interrupted forced takeovers and interrupted purges (purge resumes from its marker until the whole tree is clean). Retention: last 10 rotations pruned with the history. Doctor warns at journal > 5 MB; perf test: 10k-event replay < 1 s. **Filesystem support boundary: v1 supports local filesystems only** — lock and rename atomicity are not guaranteed on network/shared mounts (NFS/SMB/cloud-sync folders); doctor detects and warns, README states it.
62:**Root discovery & degraded modes** (Gate-1 additions, iterations 1+2): repo root = nearest ancestor containing `.handoff/` or `baton.config.json`, else `git rev-parse --show-toplevel` (worktree-aware: linked worktrees resolve to their own toplevel, shared state stays per-worktree), else cwd — `--root <path>` overrides; nested/monorepo launches therefore resolve to the same bundle deterministically, but **discovery never crosses the boundary of a nested independent git repository** (a `.git` between cwd and a candidate root stops the walk) unless `--root` says otherwise. Non-git directories: supported — `git` section is null, git-dependent features degrade with explicit warnings. Read-only sandboxes (EACCES/EROFS on `.handoff/`): checkpoint fails open with a warning naming the user-executed fallback (`baton checkpoint` run manually outside the sandbox; `receive --print-prompt` never needs writes until `--commit`). All git invocations carry a 5 s timeout (configurable); timeout → git section marked `unavailable`, never a hang.
64:**Transcript policy** (Gate-1 additions, iterations 1+2): transcript-tail capture at PreCompact is **opt-in and off by default** (`capture.transcriptTail` in `baton.config.json`), bounded (last 10 messages, 8 KB cap), passed through a secret-redaction filter (common credential/token patterns; fixtures with planted secrets prove redaction), stored in a dedicated bundle field excluded from HANDOFF.md by default. `baton purge-transcript` removes the field from **every retained copy** — active snapshot, `.bak`, journal (rewrite-and-rotate under lock), and all `history/` freezes — with a test asserting planted secrets are absent from the entire `.handoff/` tree afterward. **Diagnostics logging contract (iteration 4)**: `.handoff/log/` entries are metadata-only and redacted — raw hook payloads never enter them (fixture-proven) — with bounded retention (last 50 entries / 7 days) and inclusion in the whole-tree purge test.
66:**Checkpoint engine**: adapters send pre-normalized `baton/event@1` on stdin (the real API); raw hook payloads get best-effort per-platform extraction (`normalize.mjs`) that never throws — unknowns degrade to `note` events. Idempotent via dedupe keys; journal append always, snapshot rewrite throttled (≥10 events / 30 s / important event types). **Hook-safety rule: exit 0 on all soft failures** so a checkpoint can never break the host harness (`--strict` for CI). `finalize --reason "…" [--reason-class] [--to]` records the switch reason (class inferred from text via the classifier when omitted), refreshes git state, freezes history.
68:**Limit detector**: two tiers — **structured signals first** (Claude Code `StopFailure` matcher error types; documented limit-specific fields in codex JSON output), text signatures as fallback. **Exit-code rule (iteration 3): a generic nonzero exit never classifies as usage-limit on its own** — exit 10 requires limit-specific structured or text evidence; nonzero without evidence = `other-error`. Fixture corpus includes non-limit failing processes. Signatures are **versioned data** (`core/data/signatures.v1.json`), seeded with the verified strings for all three platforms, matcher kinds `substring`/`regex`/`json-field`, per-signature confidence, precedence usage-limit > auth > throttle > other-error, `--signatures <path>` overlay for hot-patching drift, `--explain` for auditability. Hardening (Gate-1 additions): input is ANSI-stripped before matching; the fixture corpus includes negative/near-miss cases (throttle-vs-usage confusables, multiline, malformed JSON, localized text) so false positives are regression-tested; overlay regexes are lint-checked (length cap, no backreferences, linear-time constructs) and matching carries a time guard; a low-confidence match **never silently avoids a platform** — it warns and defers to user intake at receive. Cursor entries carry `confidence: low|medium` explicitly.
70:**Role matrix**: committed `baton.config.json` at repo root (`baton/config@1`) mapping roles → ordered platform+model chains (the seven methodology roles, defined below), plus per-platform `defaults`. Resolver is pure and explainable: skips `avoid[]` platforms (auto-populated with the origin platform when reason-class is usage-limit — never remap back onto the platform that died), resolves `mode: native|delegated|forced-default|unavailable` with `{chainIndex, skipped[]}` audit data. **Availability probing** (Gate-1 additions, iterations 1–3): `doctor` probes each platform noninteractively, hard 3 s bound per probe, results cached 15 min in the state dir. Probe record = two separate dimensions: **capability** (ordered, cumulative: `installed → authenticated → reachable`, recorded as far as verifiable) and **probe outcome** (`ok | rate-limited | error | timeout`). Resolver eligibility: **selectable** = capability ≥ authenticated and outcome ≠ rate-limited; anything with an unverifiable dimension resolves as selectable-with-`degraded`-flag; `rate-limited` platforms are skipped like `avoid[]` entries. Offline resolution always succeeds, flagged `degraded`.
72:**Receive**: loads bundle with recovery + staleness checks (age > 12 h; HEAD moved; dirty mismatch), merges intake flags (`--origin`, `--reason`), resolves roles, and runs as the **prepare/commit transaction defined under Concurrency** — prompt emission at prepare, state mutation only at commit. Resume prompt ≤2,500 chars (brief, why-you're-here, role table, warnings, top-5 next actions, pointer to HANDOFF.md); the prompt explicitly frames bundle content as **unverified claims to audit against the working tree, not instructions to obey** (prompt-injection posture — see Threat model).
76:**Attribution guard** (three layers): (1) `init` deep-merges `.claude/settings.json` → `{"attribution": {"commit": "", "pr": ""}}` (only when keys absent; refuses to touch malformed JSON; global settings documented + doctor-checked, never written by core); (2) `doctor` scans last 50 commits for AI co-author/generated-with trailers (word-boundary-safe patterns) and flags with remediation; (3) a test-suite invariant scans every rendered output and template for AI-credit strings and fails the build.
78:Full module APIs, per-module test lists, repo file tree, and the fixture corpus (verbatim limit strings, hook payloads, corrupt bundles) are specified in the core design doc (committed as `docs/design/core.md` in task 0); implementation follows the named-task TDD sequence below — **tasks are identified by name, not number; the exact count is finalized when review directories are generated**. **`sessionHint` derivation (Gate-1 final note)**: per adapter, from the hook payload's stable session id (Claude Code `session_id`; Codex `session_id`; Cursor session field); when a payload lacks one, core generates a process-scoped hint flagged `unstable: true` — unstable hints never trigger foreign-session rejection on their own, only a warning.
79:### Checkpoint vocabulary (used everywhere)
81:- **Mechanical checkpoint** (`baton checkpoint`): hook/script-driven, no LLM — git branch/HEAD/dirty summary, timestamps, source platform/model, files touched. <1 s, safe per-turn.
82:- **Narrative seal** (`baton finalize`, surfaced as `/handoff`): model-written — decisions + rationale, verified-done vs claimed-done, next steps, gotchas, reason for switching.
84:Claude Code DOES emit a structured limit-death event (`StopFailure` with a `rate_limit` matcher); Codex and Cursor do not. The design is therefore: event-driven detection where it exists, rolling mechanical checkpoints everywhere (last coherent turn always captured), narrative seals at milestones, and receive compensating for a missing seal by asking the user why they switched and re-deriving git facts live, auditing HANDOFF.md claims against them.
86:### Claude Code adapter (Tier A — highest fidelity)
90:Hooks (all fail-open — errors log to `.handoff/log/`, exit 0; a checkpoint must never break a session):
94:| `StopFailure` (matcher enum — e.g. `rate_limit`, `billing_error`, `server_error` — **snapshot verbatim from the official hooks reference during task 0**; fixtures generated from that canonical list; unknown future values normalize to `unknown` safely) | **The limit-death signal.** Classifies the error (matcher + payload through `baton detect`), writes a fail-open mechanical checkpoint stamped `trigger: stop-failure/<type>`, and on `rate_limit` marks the bundle limit-hit so the next SessionStart (any platform) surfaces it. Output/exit code are ignored by the harness — safe by construction. Tests cover the full snapshot enum. | 30 s |
95:| `Stop` | Routine mechanical checkpoint per assistant turn (proven codex stop-gate pattern; never emits `decision: block`) | 30 s |
97:| `SessionEnd` | Clean-exit checkpoint | 30 s |
98:| `SessionStart` | Not a checkpoint: if a sealed or limit-hit bundle from another platform exists, inject "handoff pending — suggest `/baton:receive`" context | 10 s |
102:Commands: **`/baton:handoff`** (model-invocable so Claude can propose it near limits; writes the narrative — auditing every "done" claim against tool evidence — then seals via finalize, then prints the destination's resume command); **`/baton:receive`** (`disable-model-invocation: true`; runs receive → one AskUserQuestion with two questions: origin platform with detected option first, reason for switch → ack runs the core commit transaction (received seal archived, this session adopted as owner, fresh `open` generation; double-receive prevented by the one-time token; command tests assert the active/open + archived/received outcome) → evidence audit of bundle git snapshot vs live HEAD/status → announce role-remap table → auto-continue next step, pausing only per AGENTS.md boundaries); **`/baton:baton-setup`** (doctor + choose surfaces + init + template scaffold with diff preview). Receive-side continuation is a **skill** (`handoff-protocol`), not an agent — the main thread must inherit the task; a subagent would strand context. Optional v2 `handoff-verifier` agent (fresh-context bundle audit) is out of v1 scope.
104:### Codex adapter (Tier A− — hook-driven as of Codex >= 0.144)
106:Gate-1 correction: Codex now ships lifecycle hooks, so the adapter is redesigned around them. Surfaces: (1) **repo `.codex/hooks.json`** (written by `init --codex`, merged never clobbered): `Stop` → mechanical checkpoint, `PreCompact` → context-boundary checkpoint, `SessionStart` → pending-handoff notice via injected context. No StopFailure equivalent exists on Codex, so a limit death is bounded by the last Stop checkpoint (one turn worst case). (2) `.agents/skills/baton-handoff/SKILL.md` per the Agent Skills standard — carries the receive/handoff protocol, invoked as `$baton-handoff` or by description match. (3) Repo `AGENTS.md` Handoff Protocol section as the always-on instruction layer (narrative checkpoints after subtasks / before risky ops / at wind-down). (4) `~/.codex/prompts/*.md` demoted to **legacy fallback only** (deprecated upstream), written only with `--with-legacy-prompts`. The notify shim is **cut** — hooks make it obsolete; `~/.codex/config.toml` is never touched. **Trust gate** (verified, Gate-1 iteration 2): Codex requires per-definition review of non-managed command hooks — trust is recorded against the hook's hash, and new/changed hooks are skipped until re-trusted via `/hooks`. Setup and doctor therefore report four distinct states per hook — installed / enabled / **trusted** / observed-executing (canary event) — and the fidelity claim is gated on the canary, with setup guiding the user through `/hooks` when review is pending. Hook definitions include `commandWindows` overrides. Fidelity statement ships verbatim in README + doctor: mechanical staleness ≤ 1 turn via Stop hook **once trusted**; narrative staleness bounded by protocol compliance; git state always re-derived at receive. Requires codex-cli >= 0.144 (doctor enforces; older versions fall back to protocol-only Tier C with an explicit warning).
108:### Cursor adapter (Tier A− — hook-driven; event names verified against official docs, Gate-1 iteration 2)
110:Surfaces: `.cursor/hooks.json` (project-level, `{"version":1,"hooks":{...}}`) with the **verified** events — `stop` → mechanical checkpoint per agent turn (same semantics as Claude Code `Stop`); `afterFileEdit` → debounced checkpoint (debounce enforced by the CLI, one real checkpoint per 2 min); `beforeShellExecution` with a git-commit matcher → checkpoint before agent-issued commits (**documented limitation: commits made through the SCM UI are invisible to hooks — no pre-commit event exists**); `sessionStart` → pending-handoff notice; `preCompact` → context-boundary checkpoint. Plus `.cursor/commands/{receive,handoff,baton-setup}.md` (CLI loading unconfirmed — convenience layer; every behavior also reachable via AGENTS.md, which cursor-agent reads natively, and via the universal fallback **`baton receive --print-prompt`**) and a ≤5-line `.cursor/rules/baton-handoff.mdc` (`alwaysApply: true`). hooks.json written only after explicit confirmation, merged not clobbered; project hooks require a **trusted workspace** — doctor checks trust and gates the fidelity claim on an observed hook canary. Fidelity: mechanical staleness ≤ 1 turn via `stop` when trusted; untrusted/cloud contexts degrade to protocol-only with an explicit warning.
112:### Templates: ideal AGENTS.md + CLAUDE.md
116:AGENTS.md sections (template `templates/AGENTS.md.tpl`, `{{TEST_CMD}}`-style variables filled by `baton init` from detection): 1 Project (one paragraph + directory map) · 2 Roles and models (points at committed config; "resolve via `baton remap`, never hardcode model names") · 3 Working phases (research → plan → test-authoring → implementation → review → handoff, one-line entry/exit criteria, "state your phase") · 4 TDD contract (no implementation before failing tests exist and are verifier-approved; test changes re-enter verification; progress claims must cite command output) · 5 Review gates (Gate 1 plan, per-subtask test review, Gate 2 dual independent final review; verdicts APPROVED/APPROVED_WITH_NOTES/BLOCKED) · 6 Handoff protocol (when/how to checkpoint and resume) · 7 Boundaries and stop rules (never force-push/hard-reset/delete without explicit instruction; pause for scope change/irreversible/credentials; otherwise "when you have enough information to act, act") · 8 Validation (command table; done = green + gate verdict) · 9 Git and attribution (sole author SakshamUboweja; zero AI attribution invariant; **commit cadence rule: commit after every completed subtask/green TDD cycle — small, regular commits to main so contributions accrue to Saksham's GitHub profile**; never commit `.handoff/`) · 10 Memory (`.handoff/notes.md`, gitignored, rides the bundle across handoffs).
124:### Encoded development methodology (single committed config: `baton.config.json`)
147:When only one vendor is reachable, test-author/verifier fall back to different models in fresh sessions and every review artifact header carries `degraded: single-vendor` — weakening is auditable, never silent. Review artifacts extend the wt-* convention exactly: `reviews/<task-id>/{gate-1-plan, subtask-NN-tests, gate-2-final/{reviewer-a, reviewer-b, findings.md}}/iteration-NN/{prompt.md, verdict.md}`, templates ported from `wt-supervisor/reviews/_templates/`. The loop: plan doc in `docs/plans/` with goal + genuine constraints (falsifiable acceptance criteria) → Gate 1 → per-subtask test-first with the implementer forbidden from weakening/deleting tests (modifications re-enter verification) → dual independent fresh-context final reviews → collate `findings.md` → fix blocking items, re-review by the raising reviewer → loop until acceptance constraints are met with evidence attached. **Iteration budget (Saksham's rule): every review gate loops at most 5 times — hard stop after the 5th iteration; on exhaustion, the gate ends with the outstanding findings escalated to Saksham for a human decision instead of a 6th run.** This cap is encoded in the AGENTS.md template's review-gates section and applies to building this repo too.
149:### Install / distribution
156:| Anything else | `baton receive --print-prompt` / `baton checkpoint` (pure CLI) |
160:### Build order (TDD; one commit per subtask; sole-author commits to main)
163:1. **Core tasks** (named, dependency-ordered; one commit each): repo scaffold + CI → `util/fsx` (atomic writes) → `util/jsonl` + ids → **lock/lease module** → bundle schema → merge reducer → compact → store (recovery + rotation) → HANDOFF.md render + goldens → CLI shell + status → **git snapshot** (moved before checkpoint — checkpoints need git state) → normalize → checkpoint cmd → finalize cmd (incl. journal rotation) → signatures table → classifier (incl. negative/ANSI corpus) → detect cmd → roles matrix → resolve → remap cmd → receive prompt → receive prepare/commit cmd → gitignore + managed-block scaffold → attribution merge → init cmd → git guard + doctor → attribution invariant suite → **purge-transcript cmd** (whole-tree, interruption-recovery, packaged-bin tests) → **concurrency test suite** (dual writers, provably-dead takeover, pause-after-final-fence-check, prepare-vs-checkpoint races, competing receivers) → e2e failover test (init → 25 checkpoints → kill-mid-write sim → finalize → receive → golden prompt, plus chained A→B→C failover) → contract docs. Each commit: tests first (test-author role), verified (test-verifier role), then implementation.
164:2. **Claude Code adapter** (~5 commits): hooks.json + checkpoint/session scripts (fixture-driven tests) → commands → handoff-protocol skill → plugin manifests → install smoke test.
165:3. **Codex adapter** (~3 commits): `.codex/hooks.json` writer/merger + skills templates → init --codex writer → legacy-prompts fallback (`--with-legacy-prompts`).
172:### Acceptance constraints (the loop's exit condition)
174:1. E2E failover test passes on real fs: a session's bundle survives a kill-mid-write, finalizes with a usage-limit reason, and produces a correct remapped resume prompt — **contract-tested in all six directions** (3 platforms × 2 directions).
176:3. Fresh-machine simulation: `init` scaffolds all surfaces idempotently (double-run = all no-ops); `doctor` reports accurate per-harness status; `npm pack` + fresh-install + bin/shebang smoke tests pass in CI on Linux, macOS, and Windows.
179:6. Concurrency suite green: dual writers, provably-dead takeover with live/unknown-owner refusal, competing receivers, prepare-without-commit, chained failover.
182:### Verification
187:- `baton doctor --strict` green on this machine (attribution settings present globally — one-time `~/.claude/settings.json` edit done during setup, with Saksham's consent since it's a global file).
189:### Compatibility policy (Gate-1 addition)
191:Tested version floors, enforced/reported by `doctor`: Claude Code ≥ the version documenting `StopFailure` (verify exact floor during task 0); codex-cli ≥ 0.144 (hooks; older → protocol-only Tier C with warning); cursor-agent ≥ 2026.05. Untested-newer versions get a doctor info line, not a failure. Signature table carries its own `updated` date; hooks payload drift is absorbed by the `baton/event@1` normalization seam.
193:### Threat model (Gate-1 addition)
197:### Top risks (merged, deduplicated)
201:3. **No usage-limit event on any harness / model-compliance dependence on Codex & Cursor** — rolling mechanical checkpoints bound staleness to one turn; receive-time git re-derivation + intake questions compensate; per-tier fidelity documented honestly.
203:5. **Role-matrix rot / silent single-vendor degradation** — remap validates against live availability; `degraded:` flags in review artifacts; doctor surfaces it.

+      if (n === 'x') {

+        i += 3;
+        continue;
+      }
+      if (n === 'u') {

+          out.add(cp(parseInt(pattern.slice(i + 3, e), 16)));
+          i = e;
+          continue;
+        }
+        out.add(String.fromCharCode(parseInt(pattern.slice(i + 2, i + 6), 16)));
+        i += 5;
+        continue;
+      }
+      // Control escapes decode to their real char; escaped metacharacters (`\(`,
+      // `\.`, `\*`…) decode to the literal char (iter-4 I4 defense-in-depth: the
+      // old code dropped these, so a chained star over an escaped metachar or a
+      // `\n` literal never got a matching probe run). Class shorthands are
+      // already represented by COVER.

+      if (Object.prototype.hasOwnProperty.call(named, n)) out.add(named[/** @type {'n'} */ (n)]);
+      else if (n !== undefined && !'dwsDWSbB'.includes(n)) out.add(n);
+      i += 1;
+      continue;
+    }
+    if (!META.has(c)) out.add(c);
   }
   return [...out];

+/**
+ * Approximate character-set overlap between two simple-atom alphabets.
+ * Descriptors: {all:true} (dot / broad negated shorthand) | {sh:'d'|'w'|'s'} |
+ * {lit:Set<string>}. Returns true unless the two sets are PROVABLY disjoint —
+ * conservative for untrusted overlays (iter-4 I4).
+ * @param {any} a @param {any} b
+ */
+function alphasOverlap(a, b) {
+  if (!a || !b) return false;
+  if (a.all || b.all) return true;
+  if (a.sh && b.sh) {
+    if (a.sh === b.sh) return true;
+    // \s is disjoint from \d and \w; \d ⊂ \w so they overlap.
+    return !(a.sh === 's' || b.sh === 's');
+  }
+  if (a.lit && b.lit) {
+    for (const c of a.lit) if (b.lit.has(c)) return true;
+    return false;
+  }
+  const sh = a.sh ? a.sh : b.sh;
+  const lit = a.lit ? a.lit : b.lit;
+  for (const c of lit) {
+    if (sh === 'd' && c >= '0' && c <= '9') return true;
+    if (sh === 'w' && /[A-Za-z0-9_]/.test(c)) return true;
+    if (sh === 's' && /\s/.test(c)) return true;
+  }
+  return false;
+}
+
+/**
+ * Structural scan for a run of ≥2 CONSECUTIVE atoms, each with an UNBOUNDED
+ * quantifier (`*`, `+`, `{n,}`), whose alphabets overlap — `.*.*…`, `\d*\d*…`,
+ * `\(*\(*…`, `\n*\n*…`, `[a-z]*[a-z]*…`. Such a chain partitions the same input
+ * combinatorially and backtracks super-linearly, yet is invisible to the
+ * quantified-GROUP scan; and because a finite probe alphabet cannot guarantee a
+ * non-matching tail for every atom (`.` accepts almost everything), rejecting
+ * the STRUCTURE at load is the reliable guard (iter-4 I4, reviewer-a #7 /
+ * reviewer-b #3). Groups reset the run (the group scan covers quantified groups;
+ * a group's overlap with a simple atom is not decided here). Returns a problem
+ * string or null.
+ * @param {string} pattern
+ * @returns {string | null}
+ */
+function scanChainedQuantifiedAtoms(pattern) {
+  const p = pattern;
+  /** @type {any} */
+  let prev = null; // alpha of the previous atom IF it carried an unbounded quantifier, else null
+
+  for (let i = 0; i < p.length; ) {
+    const c = p[i];
+    // Chain-breakers: alternation and anchors end any run.
+    if (c === '|' || c === '^' || c === '$') {
+      prev = null;
+      i += 1;
+      continue;
+    }
+    /** @type {any} */
+    let alpha = null;
+    let isGroup = false;
+    if (c === '\\') {
+      const n = p[i + 1];
+      // decode length of the escape token
+      let len = 2;
+      if (n === 'x') len = 4;
+      else if (n === 'u') len = p[i + 2] === '{' ? (p.indexOf('}', i) - i + 1) : 6;
+      if (n === 'd' || n === 'w' || n === 's') alpha = { sh: n };
+      else if (n === 'D' || n === 'W' || n === 'S' || n === 'b' || n === 'B') alpha = { all: true }; // broad/negated or anchor-ish → conservative
+      else alpha = { lit: new Set([decodeEscape(p.slice(i, i + len))]) }; // \(, \n, \t, \xNN, \uNNNN → the literal char
+      i += Math.max(2, len);
+    } else if (c === '[') {
+      const end = classEnd(p, i);
+      const body = p.slice(i + 1, end);
+      alpha = body.startsWith('^') ? { all: true } : classAlpha(body);
+      i = end + 1;
+    } else if (c === '(') {
+      // Skip the whole group (balance-aware); recurse into its body for chains.
+      const end = groupEnd(p, i);
+      const inner = scanChainedQuantifiedAtoms(p.slice(i + 1, end));
+      if (inner) return inner;
+      isGroup = true;
+      i = end + 1;
+    } else if (c === '.') {
+      alpha = { all: true };
+      i += 1;
+    } else {
+      alpha = { lit: new Set([c]) };
+      i += 1;
+    }
+
+    // Read an optional quantifier and classify it as unbounded or not.
+    let unbounded = false;
+    const q = p[i];
+    if (q === '*' || q === '+') {
+      unbounded = true;
+      i += 1;
+    } else if (q === '{') {
+      const close = p.indexOf('}', i);
+      if (close !== -1) {
+        const spec = p.slice(i + 1, close);
+        // {n,} (no upper bound) is unbounded; {n} and {n,m} are bounded.
+        unbounded = /^\d+,\s*$/.test(spec);
+        i = close + 1;
+      }
+    } else if (q === '?') {
+      i += 1;
+    }
+    // consume a lazy/possessive modifier if present
+    if (p[i] === '?' || p[i] === '+') i += 1;
+
+    if (isGroup) {
+      // A group breaks the simple-atom run.
+      prev = null;
+      continue;
+    }
+    // prev + current = a run of ≥2 CONSECUTIVE overlapping unbounded-quantified
+    // atoms → super-linear backtracking. Reject.
+    if (unbounded && prev && alphasOverlap(prev, alpha)) {
+      return 'chained overlapping unbounded quantifiers (e.g. `.*.*`, `\\d*\\d*`, `\\(*\\(*`) backtrack super-linearly';
+    }
+    prev = unbounded ? alpha : null;
+  }
+  return null;
+}
+
+/** Decode a single-char escape token (`\(`, `\n`, `\t`, `\xNN`, `\uNNNN`, `\u{...}`) to its literal char. @param {string} tok */
+function decodeEscape(tok) {
+  const rest = tok.slice(1);
+  if (rest[0] === 'x') return String.fromCharCode(parseInt(rest.slice(1, 3), 16));
+  if (rest[0] === 'u') {
+    const h = rest[1] === '{' ? rest.slice(2, rest.indexOf('}')) : rest.slice(1, 5);
+    try {
+      return String.fromCodePoint(parseInt(h, 16));
+    } catch {
+      return rest[0];
+    }
+  }
+  const named = { n: '\n', t: '\t', r: '\r', f: '\f', v: '\v', 0: '\0' };
+  return named[/** @type {'n'} */ (rest[0])] ?? rest[0];
+}
+
+/** Index of the `]` closing a class opened at `open` (a `]` immediately after `[`/`[^` is a literal). @param {string} p @param {number} open */
+function classEnd(p, open) {
+  let i = open + 1;
+  if (p[i] === '^') i += 1;
+  if (p[i] === ']') i += 1; // leading ] is literal
+  for (; i < p.length; i++) {
+    if (p[i] === '\\') {
+      i += 1;
+      continue;
+    }
+    if (p[i] === ']') return i;
+  }
+  return p.length - 1;
+}
+
+/** Index of the `)` closing a group opened at `open` (balance-aware, class/escape-safe). @param {string} p @param {number} open */
+function groupEnd(p, open) {
+  let depth = 0;
+  let inClass = false;
+  for (let i = open; i < p.length; i++) {
+    const c = p[i];
+    if (c === '\\') {
+      i += 1;
+      continue;
+    }
+    if (inClass) {
+      if (c === ']') inClass = false;
+      continue;
+    }
+    if (c === '[') inClass = true;
+    else if (c === '(') depth += 1;
+    else if (c === ')') {
+      depth -= 1;
+      if (depth === 0) return i;
+    }
+  }
+  return p.length - 1;
+}
+
+/** Approximate alphabet of a (non-negated) class body: literals + range endpoints + shorthands. @param {string} body */
+function classAlpha(body) {
+  if (/\\[dwsDWS]/.test(body) || /\\[DWS]/.test(body)) return { all: true }; // shorthand inside a class → treat broadly
+  /** @type {Set<string>} */
+  const lit = new Set();
+  for (let i = 0; i < body.length; i++) {
+    if (body[i] === '\\') {
+      lit.add(decodeEscape(body.slice(i, i + 2)));
+      i += 1;
+      continue;
+    }
+    // range a-z: add endpoints (a run of an endpoint char backtracks the same)
+    if (body[i + 1] === '-' && body[i + 2] !== undefined) {
+      lit.add(body[i]);
+      lit.add(body[i + 2]);
+      i += 2;
+      continue;
+    }
+    lit.add(body[i]);
+  }
+  return { lit };
+}
+
 const ALLOWED_FLAGS = 'dgimsuvy';

+  const problem = scanQuantifiedGroups(pattern) || scanChainedQuantifiedAtoms(pattern);
   if (problem) {
     throw new Error(`signature ${id}: ${problem} (pathological backtracking / non-linear-time risk)`);

     1	import { atomicWriteJson } from '../util/fsx.mjs';
     2	import { appendEntry, readAllTolerant } from '../util/jsonl.mjs';

     6	/**
     7	 * Operation-scoped mutation lock (plan §Concurrency, Lock model).
     8	 * Acquire = atomic non-recursive mkdir of .handoff/lock/; exclusivity comes from
     9	 * EEXIST. Ownership facts live in owner.json; logical session ownership lives in
    10	 * bundle.json, never here. Takeover only when the owner is provably dead on this
    11	 * host — every other contended state refuses, and `force` never changes that.
    12	 */
    13	
    14	export class LockHeldError extends Error {
    15	  constructor(/** @type {string} */ message) {
    16	    super(message);
    17	    this.name = 'LockHeldError';
    18	  }
    19	}

    21	export class FencingError extends Error {
    22	  constructor(/** @type {string} */ message) {
    23	    super(message);
    24	    this.name = 'FencingError';
    25	  }
    26	}

    28	/** @param {string} root */
    29	function lockPaths(root) {
    30	  const dir = `${root}/.handoff`;
    31	  return {
    32	    dir,
    33	    lockDir: `${dir}/lock`,
    34	    owner: `${dir}/lock/owner.json`,
    35	    journal: `${dir}/journal.ndjson`,
    36	  };
    37	}
    38	
    39	const ESCAPE_HATCH =
    40	  'Verify manually that no baton process still holds it, then delete .handoff/lock to clear.';
    41	
    42	/**
    43	 * Inspect the current lock state.
    44	 * @param {any} io @param {ReturnType<typeof lockPaths>} p
    45	 * @returns {{state: 'free'} | {state: 'torn', cause: string} | {state: 'live'|'dead'|'cross-host', owner: any}}
    46	 */
    47	function inspect(io, p) {
    48	  if (!io.fs.existsSync(p.lockDir)) return { state: 'free' };
    49	  if (!io.fs.existsSync(p.owner)) {
    50	    return { state: 'torn', cause: 'owner.json is missing (torn lock metadata)' };
    51	  }
    52	  let owner;
    53	  try {
    54	    owner = JSON.parse(io.fs.readFileSync(p.owner, 'utf8'));
    55	  } catch {
    56	    return { state: 'torn', cause: 'owner.json is unparseable (torn lock metadata)' };
    57	  }
    58	  if (owner.host !== io.host) return { state: 'cross-host', owner };
    59	  if (io.processAlive(owner.pid, owner.startTime)) return { state: 'live', owner };
    60	  return { state: 'dead', owner };
    61	}
    62	
    63	/** @param {any} io @param {ReturnType<typeof lockPaths>} p @param {string} text */
    64	function journalNote(io, p, text) {
    65	  io.fs.mkdirSync(p.dir, { recursive: true });
    66	  // Allocate seq + dedupeKey like every other journal entry (gate-2 fix,
    67	  // reviewer-b finding 2): a keyless, seq-less note poisons journalSeq to NaN
    68	  // on replay and dedupes every later keyless event. We are already inside the
    69	  // lock here, so reading the tail for allocation is race-free.
    70	  let tailSeq = 0;
    71	  try {
    72	    const snap = JSON.parse(io.fs.readFileSync(`${p.dir}/bundle.json`, 'utf8'));
    73	    if (typeof snap?.journalSeq === 'number') tailSeq = snap.journalSeq;
    74	  } catch {
    75	    // no snapshot yet — journal tail below still counts
    76	  }
    77	  try {
    78	    for (const e of readAllTolerant(io.fs, p.journal).entries) {
    79	      if (typeof e.seq === 'number' && e.seq > tailSeq) tailSeq = e.seq;
    80	    }
    81	  } catch {
    82	    // no journal yet
    83	  }
    84	  const ts = io.now();
    85	  appendEntry(io.fs, p.journal, {
    86	    seq: tailSeq + 1,
    87	    ts,
    88	    type: 'note',
    89	    dedupeKey: dedupeKey({ ts, type: 'note', source: 'lock', text }),
    90	    writerId: ['lock', io.host, io.pid].join('-'),
    91	    source: 'lock',
    92	    payload: { text },
    93	  });
    94	}
    95	
    96	/** @param {any} io @param {ReturnType<typeof lockPaths>} p */
    97	function publishOwner(io, p) {
    98	  const token = io.newFencingToken();
    99	  atomicWriteJson(io.fs, p.owner, {
   100	    host: io.host,
   101	    pid: io.pid,
   102	    startTime: io.startTime,
   103	    fencingToken: token,
   104	    acquiredAt: io.now(),
   105	    heartbeatAt: io.now(),
   106	  });
   107	  return token;
   108	}
   109	
   110	/** @param {any} io @param {ReturnType<typeof lockPaths>} p */
   111	function acquire(io, p) {
   112	  io.fs.mkdirSync(p.dir, { recursive: true });
   113	  try {
   114	    io.fs.mkdirSync(p.lockDir);
   115	    return publishOwner(io, p);
   116	  } catch (err) {
   117	    if (/** @type {any} */ (err)?.code !== 'EEXIST') throw err;
   118	  }
   119	
   120	  const s = inspect(io, p);
   121	  switch (s.state) {
   122	    case 'free':
   123	      // Raced a release between mkdir failure and inspection; retry once. A
   124	      // competitor can win THIS mkdir too (gate-2 fix 12: observed under real
   125	      // barrier-released contention) — that is contention, not corruption.
   126	      try {
   127	        io.fs.mkdirSync(p.lockDir);
   128	      } catch (err) {
   129	        if (/** @type {any} */ (err)?.code === 'EEXIST') {
   130	          throw new LockHeldError('lock was re-acquired by a competitor mid-inspection — transient contention');
   131	        }
   132	        throw err;
   133	      }
   134	      return publishOwner(io, p);
   135	    case 'live':
   136	      throw new LockHeldError(
   137	        `lock is held by a live process (pid ${s.owner.pid} on ${s.owner.host}) — terminate that process first.`,
   138	      );
   139	    case 'cross-host':
   140	      throw new Error(
   141	        `lock is held from another host ('${s.owner.host}', this is '${io.host}') — cross-host locks are never provably safe to steal. ${ESCAPE_HATCH}`,
   142	      );
   143	    case 'torn':
   144	      // During ACQUIRE, torn metadata is usually a competitor caught between
   145	      // its mkdir and owner.json publication — transient by construction, so
   146	      // LockHeldError lets withLock wait it out (gate-2 fix 12). A genuinely
   147	      // crashed publisher exhausts the retry budget and surfaces this same
   148	      // message; recovery semantics (recoverLock) are unchanged: never steal.
   149	      throw new LockHeldError(`${s.cause} — unsupported for automatic recovery. ${ESCAPE_HATCH}`);
   150	    case 'dead': {
   151	      // Atomic dead-lock reclaim (gate-2 iter-2 B1, hardened iter-3 finding-1).
   152	      // rm-then-mkdir is NOT mutually exclusive: two contenders can both observe
   153	      // 'dead' before either removes the stale dir, then both rm+mkdir — the
   154	      // second silently clobbers the first, yielding two simultaneous holders and
   155	      // two takeover notes. rename() is the atomic arbiter instead: the stale
   156	      // lock dir has exactly one name, so only one process can move it aside (the
   157	      // rest get ENOENT). Winning the rename IS winning the reclaim; a contender
   158	      // that recreated the dir via the 'free' path first makes our mkdir EEXIST →
   159	      // transient contention. The fencing token stays the write-time backstop.
   160	      const aside = `${p.lockDir}.reclaim-${io.pid}-${io.newFencingToken()}`;
   161	      try {
   162	        io.fs.renameSync(p.lockDir, aside);
   163	      } catch (err) {
   164	        // Source already gone → another contender is reclaiming it; wait+retry.
   165	        if (/** @type {any} */ (err)?.code === 'ENOENT') {
   166	          throw new LockHeldError('a competitor is reclaiming the dead lock — transient contention');
   167	        }
   168	        throw err;
   169	      }
   170	      io.fs.rmSync(aside, { recursive: true, force: true });
   171	      try {
   172	        io.fs.mkdirSync(p.lockDir);
   173	      } catch (err) {
   174	        if (/** @type {any} */ (err)?.code === 'EEXIST') {
   175	          throw new LockHeldError('a competitor re-claimed the dead lock first — transient contention');
   176	        }
   177	        throw err;
   178	      }
   179	      const token = publishOwner(io, p);
   180	      // Journal the takeover only now — we HOLD the lock (fresh dir + published
   181	      // owner), so the note's seq is allocated while holding it (plan
   182	      // §Concurrency "sequence numbers are allocated under the lock"), never
   183	      // racing a free-path competitor's concurrent append (iter-3 F2). The note
   184	      // is best-effort (0-or-1) and MUST NOT abort acquisition (iter-4 I7): if it
   185	      // threw here — after publishOwner, before the token is returned to withLock
   186	      // — the release finally would never run and the lock would leak with a live
   187	      // owner. Swallow it; the reclaim already succeeded.
   188	      try {
   189	        journalNote(io, p, `lock takeover: took over stale lock from dead pid ${s.owner.pid} (start time ${s.owner.startTime})`);
   190	      } catch {
   191	        // audit note is best-effort
   192	      }
   193	      return token;
   194	    }
   195	    /* c8 ignore next 2 */
   196	    default:
   197	      throw new Error('unreachable lock state');
   198	  }
   199	}
   200	
   201	/**
   202	 * Fence-guarded release: the lock dir is removed only while owner.json still
   203	 * carries OUR token. A holder that paused past a takeover must not delete the
   204	 * competitor's lock on its way out — release is a write like any other.
   205	 * @param {any} io @param {ReturnType<typeof lockPaths>} p @param {string} token
   206	 */
   207	function release(io, p, token) {
   208	  try {
   209	    const current = JSON.parse(io.fs.readFileSync(p.owner, 'utf8'));
   210	    if (current.fencingToken !== token) return; // a competitor took over — their lock, not ours
   211	  } catch {
   212	    return; // owner metadata gone or torn mid-hold — never guess; leave it for recovery
   213	  }
   214	  io.fs.rmSync(p.lockDir, { recursive: true, force: true });
   215	}
   216	
   217	// A live same-host holder is transient by design — operation-scoped locks are
   218	// held for milliseconds — so contention waits instead of dropping the
   219	// operation (gate-2 fix 12: simultaneous checkpoints must all land). The budget
   220	// is generous (≈15 s): under heavy load a writer may be starved of scheduling
   221	// for seconds, and waiting is strictly safer than silently dropping its work.
   222	// Every other contended state (torn, cross-host) stays fail-fast: waiting
   223	// cannot make those provably safe.
   224	const DEFAULT_RETRY = { attempts: 600, delayMs: 25 };
   225	
   226	/** Synchronous sleep without burning a core: Atomics.wait on a throwaway buffer. */
   227	const sleepMs = (/** @type {number} */ ms) => {
   228	  Atomics.wait(new Int32Array(new SharedArrayBuffer(4)), 0, 0, ms);
   229	};
   230	
   231	/**
   232	 * Acquire → run fn(token) → release (also on throw). Synchronous by design:
   233	 * everything the lock guards is sync fs work. Live-held locks are retried on
   234	 * a bounded budget (override via io.lockRetry = {attempts, delayMs}).
   235	 * @param {string} root @param {any} io @param {(token: string) => any} fn
   236	 */
   237	export function withLock(root, io, fn) {
   238	  const p = lockPaths(root);
   239	  // Jail BEFORE any lock mutation (gate-2 iter-2 B2): a symlinked .handoff or
   240	  // .handoff/lock must never be created into or removed through — that would
   241	  // reach outside the repository. Checked once here, ahead of acquire.
   242	  const safe = checkHandoffTree(root, io);
   243	  if (!safe.ok) throw new Error(`${safe.problem} — refusing to acquire the lock`);
   244	  const retry = io.lockRetry ?? DEFAULT_RETRY;
   245	  /** @type {string} */
   246	  let token;
   247	  for (let attempt = 0; ; attempt++) {
   248	    try {
   249	      token = acquire(io, p);
   250	      break;
   251	    } catch (err) {
   252	      if (err instanceof LockHeldError && attempt < retry.attempts) {
   253	        sleepMs(retry.delayMs);
   254	        continue;
   255	      }
   256	      throw err;
   257	    }
   258	  }
   259	  try {
   260	    return fn(token);
   261	  } finally {
   262	    release(io, p, token);
   263	  }
   264	}
   265	
   266	/**
   267	 * Fast-abort layer: re-read the published token immediately before a write; a
   268	 * holder that paused past its last check aborts instead of clobbering.
   269	 * @param {string} root @param {any} io @param {string} token @param {() => any} fn
   270	 */
   271	export function guardedWrite(root, io, token, fn) {
   272	  const p = lockPaths(root);
   273	  let current = null;
   274	  try {
   275	    current = JSON.parse(io.fs.readFileSync(p.owner, 'utf8'));
   276	  } catch {
   277	    throw new FencingError('lock owner metadata is gone — fencing check failed; aborting write');
   278	  }
   279	  if (current.fencingToken !== token) {
   280	    throw new FencingError(
   281	      `fencing token changed (held '${token}', current '${current.fencingToken}') — another process took over; aborting write`,
   282	    );
   283	  }
   284	  return fn();
   285	}
   286	
   287	/**
   288	 * Explicit recovery. `force` is an intent flag only — it never changes the
   289	 * decision: exactly one state recovers (provably-dead same-host owner).
   290	 * @param {string} root @param {any} io @param {{force?: boolean}} [opts]
   291	 * @returns {{recovered: boolean, refusedReason: string | null}}
   292	 */
   293	export function recoverLock(root, io, opts = {}) {
   294	  void opts;
   295	  const p = lockPaths(root);
   296	  // Jail before recovery (gate-2 iter-2 B2): recoverLock removes the lock dir,
   297	  // so a symlinked managed tree must be refused first — even with force, a
   298	  // symlink is never provably safe to delete through.
   299	  const safe = checkHandoffTree(root, io);
   300	  if (!safe.ok) return { recovered: false, refusedReason: `${safe.problem} — refusing recovery` };
   301	  const s = inspect(io, p);
   302	  switch (s.state) {
   303	    case 'free':
   304	      return { recovered: true, refusedReason: null };
   305	    case 'dead': {
   306	      // Reclaim atomically via a single acquire() attempt + release, NOT an
   307	      // inspect-then-rmSync (iter-4 I1): the old bare rmSync had a TOCTOU — a
   308	      // competitor could win the rename-arbitrated dead-reclaim between this
   309	      // inspect() and the rmSync, publish a LIVE owner, and recovery would then
   310	      // delete a lock whose owner is alive. acquire() re-inspects atomically and
   311	      // shares the SAME rename arbitration, so recovery and a concurrent takeover
   312	      // are mutually exclusive: acquire either reclaims a still-dead lock (and
   313	      // journals the takeover under the lock) or throws because it is now
   314	      // live/torn — in which case we refuse rather than steal. release() then
   315	      // leaves the slot clear.
   316	      let token;
   317	      try {
   318	        token = acquire(io, p);
   319	      } catch (err) {
   320	        return { recovered: false, refusedReason: /** @type {any} */ (err)?.message ?? String(err) };
   321	      }
   322	      release(io, p, token);
   323	      return { recovered: true, refusedReason: null };
   324	    }
   325	    case 'live':
   326	      return {
   327	        recovered: false,
   328	        refusedReason: `lock is held by a live process (pid ${s.owner.pid} on ${s.owner.host}) — terminate that process first.`,
   329	      };
   330	    case 'cross-host':
   331	      return {
   332	        recovered: false,
   333	        refusedReason: `lock is held from another host ('${s.owner.host}', this is '${io.host}') — cross-host locks are never provably safe to steal, even with force. ${ESCAPE_HATCH}`,
   334	      };
   335	    case 'torn':
   336	      return {
   337	        recovered: false,
   338	        refusedReason: `${s.cause} — unsupported for automatic recovery, even with force. ${ESCAPE_HATCH}`,
   339	      };
   340	    /* c8 ignore next 2 */
   341	    default:
   342	      return { recovered: false, refusedReason: 'unreachable lock state' };
   343	  }
   344	}
     1	import { describe, it } from 'node:test';
     2	import assert from 'node:assert/strict';
     3	import { makeIo } from '../helpers/fakeio.mjs';
     4	import { withLock, recoverLock } from '../../core/src/bundle/lock.mjs';
     5	
     6	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
     7	// Gate-2 iteration-2 findings B1 + B2 (reviewer-a #3, #4): the dead-lock
     8	// takeover must atomically RE-CLAIM the lock dir (not just republish owner.json
     9	// into the existing one), and every lock/recover mutation must pass the
    10	// managed-tree jail first so a symlinked .handoff/lock cannot be followed
    11	// outside the repository.
    12	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    13	
    14	const OWNER = '/repo/.handoff/lock/owner.json';
    15	const deadOwner = JSON.stringify({ host: 'host-A', pid: 999, startTime: 5, fencingToken: 'STALE', acquiredAt: 'x', heartbeatAt: 'x' });
    16	
    17	describe('dead-takeover re-claims the lock dir atomically (B1)', () => {
    18	  it('takeover renames the stale lock dir ASIDE then claims a fresh one (never in-place rm)', () => {
    19	    // Only pid 4242 (this io) is alive → pid 999 is provably dead. The reclaim
    20	    // must be arbitrated by an atomic rename of the SOURCE lock dir, not an
    21	    // rm-then-mkdir that two contenders could both perform (iter-3 finding-1).
    22	    // We trace lock-dir ops in order: the CLAIM must be rename→(rm aside)→mkdir,
    23	    // with no in-place rm of the live /lock before the fresh mkdir. (The final
    24	    // rm of /lock at release, AFTER the mkdir, is correct and expected.)
    25	    const io = makeIo({ files: { [OWNER]: deadOwner } });
    26	    const LOCK = '/repo/.handoff/lock';
    27	    /** @type {string[]} */
    28	    const trace = [];
    29	    const realRename = io.fs.renameSync.bind(io.fs);
    30	    const realRm = io.fs.rmSync.bind(io.fs);
    31	    const realMkdir = io.fs.mkdirSync.bind(io.fs);
    32	    io.fs.renameSync = (from, to) => {
    33	      if (String(from) === LOCK) trace.push(`rename:${/\/lock\.reclaim-/.test(String(to)) ? 'aside' : 'other'}`);
    34	      return realRename(from, to);
    35	    };
    36	    io.fs.rmSync = (p, opts) => {
    37	      if (String(p) === LOCK) trace.push('rm:lock');
    38	      else if (/\/lock\.reclaim-/.test(String(p))) trace.push('rm:aside');
    39	      return realRm(p, opts);
    40	    };
    41	    io.fs.mkdirSync = (p, opts) => {
    42	      if (String(p) === LOCK && !(opts && opts.recursive)) trace.push('mkdir:lock');
    43	      return realMkdir(p, opts);
    44	    };
    45	    const token = withLock('/repo', io, (t) => t);
    46	
    47	    // Trace shape: mkdir:lock (initial EEXIST probe) → rename:aside → rm:aside →
    48	    // mkdir:lock (the winning claim) → rm:lock (release, AFTER the claim).
    49	    const claimMkdir = trace.lastIndexOf('mkdir:lock');
    50	    const renameAt = trace.indexOf('rename:aside');
    51	    const rmAsideAt = trace.indexOf('rm:aside');
    52	    assert.ok(renameAt >= 0, `the stale lock dir is moved aside by an atomic rename; trace ${JSON.stringify(trace)}`);
    53	    assert.ok(renameAt < rmAsideAt && rmAsideAt < claimMkdir, 'the claim order is rename-aside → rm-aside → fresh mkdir');
    54	    const beforeClaim = trace.slice(0, claimMkdir);
    55	    assert.equal(beforeClaim.includes('rm:lock'), false, 'the live lock dir is never rm-ed in place during the claim (only renamed aside)');
    56	    assert.ok(typeof token === 'string' && token.length > 0, 'a fresh token is published after the atomic claim');
    57	  });
    58	
    59	  it('a contender that loses the rename sees ENOENT-as-contention, not a second takeover', () => {
    60	    // Simulate the rename losing the race: the stale dir is already gone when we
    61	    // try to move it aside → LockHeldError (retryable), never a duplicate claim.
    62	    const io = makeIo({ files: { [OWNER]: deadOwner }, lockRetry: { attempts: 0, delayMs: 0 } });
    63	    const realRename = io.fs.renameSync.bind(io.fs);
    64	    io.fs.renameSync = (from, to) => {
    65	      if (String(from).endsWith('/lock') && /\/lock\.reclaim-/.test(String(to))) {
    66	        throw Object.assign(new Error('ENOENT'), { code: 'ENOENT' });
    67	      }
    68	      return realRename(from, to);
    69	    };
    70	    assert.throws(() => withLock('/repo', io, (t) => t), /reclaiming the dead lock|transient contention/i);
    71	  });
    72	
    73	  it('the takeover note is journaled while the lock dir is HELD — seq allocated under the lock (iter-3 F2)', () => {
    74	    // Deterministic white-box guard for the under-lock-collision finding: the
    75	    // old code journaled the takeover during arbitration (dir renamed aside, NOT
    76	    // held), so its seq could collide with a free-path competitor's concurrent
    77	    // append. Assert the note is appended only while the canonical lock dir
    78	    // exists (held). Fails against journal-before-lock, which appends it while
    79	    // the dir is renamed away.
    80	    const io = makeIo({ files: { [OWNER]: deadOwner, '/repo/.handoff/bundle.json': '{"journalSeq":0}' } });
    81	    const LOCK = '/repo/.handoff/lock';
    82	    let heldAtNoteTime = null;
    83	    const realAppend = io.fs.appendFileSync.bind(io.fs);
    84	    io.fs.appendFileSync = (p, data) => {
    85	      if (String(p).endsWith('/journal.ndjson') && /took over stale lock/.test(String(data))) {
    86	        heldAtNoteTime = io.fs.existsSync(LOCK);
    87	      }
    88	      return realAppend(p, data);
    89	    };
    90	    withLock('/repo', io, (t) => t);
    91	    assert.equal(heldAtNoteTime, true, 'the takeover note is appended while the lock dir is held');
    92	  });
    93	
    94	  it('recoverLock reclaims atomically and journals under the HELD lock (iter-3 F2 / iter-4 I1)', () => {
    95	    // recoverLock now reuses the atomic acquire (rename-arbitrated dead-reclaim)
    96	    // rather than a bare rmSync, so its audit note is the takeover note acquire
    97	    // writes AFTER publishOwner — under the held lock (seq allocated safely).
    98	    const io = makeIo({ files: { [OWNER]: deadOwner, '/repo/.handoff/bundle.json': '{"journalSeq":0}' } });
    99	    const LOCK = '/repo/.handoff/lock';
   100	    let heldAtNoteTime = null;
   101	    const realAppend = io.fs.appendFileSync.bind(io.fs);
   102	    io.fs.appendFileSync = (p, data) => {
   103	      if (String(p).endsWith('/journal.ndjson') && /took over stale lock|lock recovery/.test(String(data))) {
   104	        heldAtNoteTime = io.fs.existsSync(LOCK);
   105	      }
   106	      return realAppend(p, data);
   107	    };
   108	    const r = recoverLock('/repo', io, { force: true });
   109	    assert.equal(r.recovered, true, 'a provably-dead lock is recovered');
   110	    assert.equal(heldAtNoteTime, true, 'the recovery/takeover note is appended while the lock dir is held, never after a bare rmSync');
   111	    assert.equal(io.fs.existsSync(LOCK), false, 'the lock is released after recovery');
   112	  });
   113	
   114	  it('recoverLock refuses (no steal) when a competitor reclaims the dead lock between inspect and acquire (iter-4 I1)', () => {
   115	    // TOCTOU guard: recoverLock's outer inspect sees the DEAD owner, but by the
   116	    // time acquire() re-inspects, a competitor has published a LIVE owner. The
   117	    // old inspect-then-rmSync would delete that live lock; the acquire-based
   118	    // path re-inspects atomically, sees live, and refuses. We flip owner.json to
   119	    // a live pid on acquire's inspect (the SECOND read of owner.json — the first
   120	    // is recoverLock's own outer inspect).
   121	    // pid 4242 is alive, 999 (the seeded dead owner) is not — so the outer
   122	    // inspect sees dead and acquire's re-inspect sees the injected live owner.
   123	    const io = makeIo({ files: { [OWNER]: deadOwner, '/repo/.handoff/bundle.json': '{"journalSeq":0}' }, processAlive: (pid) => pid === 4242 });
   124	    const LIVE = JSON.stringify({ host: 'host-A', pid: 4242, startTime: 1, fencingToken: 'LIVE', acquiredAt: 'x', heartbeatAt: 'x' });
   125	    let ownerReads = 0;
   126	    const realRead = io.fs.readFileSync.bind(io.fs);
   127	    io.fs.readFileSync = (p, enc) => {
   128	      if (String(p).endsWith('/lock/owner.json')) {
   129	        ownerReads += 1;
   130	        if (ownerReads >= 2) return LIVE; // acquire's re-inspection sees a live owner
   131	      }
   132	      return realRead(p, enc);
   133	    };
   134	    const r = recoverLock('/repo', io, { force: true });
   135	    assert.equal(r.recovered, false, 'a lock that became live during recovery is not stolen');
   136	    assert.match(r.refusedReason, /live process|held/i);
   137	    assert.equal(io.fs.existsSync('/repo/.handoff/lock'), true, 'the live lock is left intact');
   138	  });
   139	
   140	  it('a takeover journalNote failure does NOT abort acquisition or leak the lock (iter-4 I7)', () => {
   141	    // journalNote runs after publishOwner but before the token returns to
   142	    // withLock. Without the try/catch, a throw there escapes acquire() → the
   143	    // release finally never runs → the lock leaks with a live owner. Make the
   144	    // note append THROW and assert acquisition still completes and releases.
   145	    const io = makeIo({ files: { [OWNER]: deadOwner, '/repo/.handoff/bundle.json': '{"journalSeq":0}' } });
   146	    const realAppend = io.fs.appendFileSync.bind(io.fs);
   147	    io.fs.appendFileSync = (p, data) => {
   148	      if (String(p).endsWith('/journal.ndjson') && /took over stale lock/.test(String(data))) {
   149	        throw new Error('simulated disk-full on the audit note');
   150	      }
   151	      return realAppend(p, data);
   152	    };
   153	    let token;
   154	    assert.doesNotThrow(() => {
   155	      token = withLock('/repo', io, (t) => t);
   156	    }, 'a note-write failure must not abort the reclaim');
   157	    assert.ok(token, 'the token is returned despite the note failure');
   158	    assert.equal(io.fs.existsSync('/repo/.handoff/lock'), false, 'the lock is released (no leak)');
   159	  });
   160	
   161	  it('after release the lock is free and the next acquisition mints its own token', () => {
   162	    const io = makeIo({ files: { [OWNER]: deadOwner } });
   163	    const first = withLock('/repo', io, (t) => t);
   164	    assert.ok(first, 'first contender takes over the dead lock');
   165	    assert.equal(io.fs.existsSync('/repo/.handoff/lock'), false, 'lock released after the operation');
   166	    const second = withLock('/repo', io, (t) => t);
   167	    assert.ok(second && second !== first, 'the next acquisition gets its own fresh token');
   168	  });
   169	});
   170	
   171	describe('lock/recover refuse a symlinked managed tree before mutating (B2)', () => {
   172	  function symlinkedLockIo() {
   173	    // The lock dir exists inside the managed tree (so the jail walk encounters
   174	    // it) but is a symlink pointing outside the repo: lstat reports a link.
   175	    const io = makeIo({ files: { '/repo/.handoff/bundle.json': '{}', '/repo/.handoff/lock/owner.json': deadOwner } });
   176	    const realLstat = io.fs.lstatSync.bind(io.fs);
   177	    io.fs.lstatSync = (p) => {
   178	      if (String(p) === '/repo/.handoff/lock') return { ...realLstat('/repo/.handoff'), isSymbolicLink: () => true, isDirectory: () => true, isFile: () => false };
   179	      return realLstat(p);
   180	    };
   181	    return io;
   182	  }
   183	
   184	  it('withLock refuses to acquire through a symlinked lock dir', () => {
   185	    const io = symlinkedLockIo();
   186	    let rmCalled = false;
   187	    const realRm = io.fs.rmSync.bind(io.fs);
   188	    io.fs.rmSync = (p, opts) => {
   189	      rmCalled = true;
   190	      return realRm(p, opts);
   191	    };
   192	    assert.throws(() => withLock('/repo', io, () => 'nope'), /symlink|jail|unsafe/i);
   193	    assert.equal(rmCalled, false, 'nothing is removed through the link');
   194	  });
   195	
   196	  it('recoverLock refuses a symlinked lock dir even with --force', () => {
   197	    const io = symlinkedLockIo();
   198	    const r = recoverLock('/repo', io, { force: true });
   199	    assert.equal(r.recovered, false);
   200	    assert.match(r.refusedReason, /symlink|jail|unsafe/i);
   201	  });
   202	});
     1	import { describe, it } from 'node:test';
     2	import assert from 'node:assert/strict';
     3	import { makeIo } from '../helpers/fakeio.mjs';
     4	import { cmdRecover } from '../../core/src/commands/recover.mjs';
     5	
     6	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
     7	// Gate-2 fix 11 (reviewer-a finding 14): `recover` sat in the frozen command
     8	// list returning not-implemented. cmdRecover wraps recoverLock and keeps its
     9	// one hard rule: exactly ONE state recovers — a provably-dead same-host owner.
    10	// `--force` is an intent flag only; live, cross-host, and torn states refuse
    11	// with the same reasons, force or not.
    12	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    13	
    14	const OWNER = (over = {}) => JSON.stringify({ host: 'host-A', pid: 999, startTime: 5, fencingToken: 't', acquiredAt: 'x', heartbeatAt: 'x', ...over });
    15	
    16	describe('cmdRecover — the one recoverable state', () => {
    17	  it('free lock: exit 0, nothing to do', async () => {
    18	    const io = makeIo({ files: { '/repo/.handoff/bundle.json': '{}' } });
    19	    assert.equal(await cmdRecover([], io), 0);
    20	    assert.match(io.stdoutText(), /recovered|free|nothing/i);
    21	  });
    22	
    23	  it('provably-dead same-host owner: cleared, journal note recorded, exit 0', async () => {
    24	    const io = makeIo({ files: { '/repo/.handoff/lock/owner.json': OWNER() } });
    25	    // default processAlive: only THIS io's pid/startTime is alive -> 999 is dead
    26	    assert.equal(await cmdRecover([], io), 0);
    27	    assert.equal(io.fs.existsSync('/repo/.handoff/lock'), false, 'stale lock removed');
    28	    // recoverLock now clears the dead lock via the atomic acquire path (iter-4
    29	    // I1), whose audit note records the takeover-of-the-stale-lock.
    30	    assert.match(io.files()['/repo/.handoff/journal.ndjson'] ?? '', /recovery|took over stale lock/i);
    31	  });
    32	
    33	  it('live same-host owner: refused with exit 1, EVEN with --force', async () => {
    34	    const files = { '/repo/.handoff/lock/owner.json': OWNER() };
    35	    const alive = () => true;
    36	    const io = makeIo({ files, processAlive: alive });
    37	    assert.equal(await cmdRecover([], io), 1);
    38	    assert.match(io.stderrText(), /live process/i);
    39	    assert.equal(io.fs.existsSync('/repo/.handoff/lock'), true, 'the live owner keeps its lock');
    40	
    41	    const io2 = makeIo({ files, processAlive: alive });
    42	    assert.equal(await cmdRecover(['--force'], io2), 1, 'force never overrides a live owner');
    43	    assert.equal(io2.fs.existsSync('/repo/.handoff/lock'), true);
    44	  });
    45	
    46	  it('cross-host owner: refused, exit 1', async () => {
    47	    const io = makeIo({ files: { '/repo/.handoff/lock/owner.json': OWNER({ host: 'other-host' }) } });
    48	    assert.equal(await cmdRecover(['--force'], io), 1);
    49	    assert.match(io.stderrText(), /another host/i);
    50	  });
    51	
    52	  it('torn metadata (lock dir without owner.json): refused, exit 1', async () => {
    53	    const io = makeIo({ files: {} });
    54	    io.fs.mkdirSync('/repo/.handoff/lock', { recursive: true });
    55	    assert.equal(await cmdRecover([], io), 1);
    56	    assert.match(io.stderrText(), /torn|unsupported/i);
    57	  });
    58	
    59	  it('--json emits exactly one envelope for both outcomes', async () => {
    60	    const io = makeIo({ files: { '/repo/.handoff/lock/owner.json': OWNER() } });
    61	    assert.equal(await cmdRecover(['--json'], io), 0);
    62	    const env = JSON.parse(io.stdoutText());
    63	    assert.equal(env.ok, true);
    64	    assert.equal(env.data.recovered, true);
    65	
    66	    const io2 = makeIo({ files: { '/repo/.handoff/lock/owner.json': OWNER({ host: 'other' }) } });
    67	    assert.equal(await cmdRecover(['--json'], io2), 1);
    68	    const env2 = JSON.parse(io2.stdoutText());
    69	    assert.equal(env2.ok, false);
    70	    assert.equal(env2.data?.recovered ?? false, false);
    71	  });
