    32	describe('lint validates the pattern+flags pair (B3)', () => {
    33	  it('rejects an unsupported regex flag at load', () => {
    34	    assert.throws(() => loadWith({ matcher: { kind: 'regex', pattern: 'usage limit', flags: 'z' } }), /ov/);
    35	  });
    36	
    37	  it('rejects duplicate flags at load', () => {
    38	    assert.throws(() => loadWith({ matcher: { kind: 'regex', pattern: 'usage limit', flags: 'ii' } }), /ov/);
    39	  });
    40	
    41	  it('accepts a valid flag combination', () => {
    42	    const t = loadWith({ matcher: { kind: 'regex', pattern: 'usage limit', flags: 'im' } });
    43	    assert.ok(t.signatures.some((s) => s.id === 'ov'));
    44	  });
    45	
    46	  it('probeRegexSafe reports a compile error distinctly from a timeout', () => {
    47	    const bad = probeRegexSafe('usage', 'z');
    48	    assert.equal(bad.safe, false);
    49	    assert.match(bad.reason, /flag|compile|invalid/i);
    50	    assert.doesNotMatch(bad.reason, /deadline|timed|pathological/i, 'a bad flag is a compile error, not a hang');
    51	  });
    52	});

    54	describe('probe catches group-free pathological patterns over non-alnum classes (M8)', () => {
    55	  const HANGS = [
    56	    ['\\d chained star', '\\d*\\d*\\d*\\d*\\d*\\d*\\d*\\d*\\d*\\d*\\d*\\d*!'],
    57	    ['\\s chained star', '\\s*\\s*\\s*\\s*\\s*\\s*\\s*\\s*\\s*\\s*\\s*\\s*!'],
    58	    ['punctuation class chained star', '[!-/]*[!-/]*[!-/]*[!-/]*[!-/]*[!-/]*[!-/]*[!-/]*[!-/]*[!-/]*X'],
    59	    // iter-3 F7: non-ASCII literal and class — the ASCII-only cover missed these.
    60	    ['non-ASCII literal chained star', 'é*é*é*é*é*é*é*é*é*é*é*é*!'],
    61	    ['non-ASCII escaped class-range chained star', '[\\u00e0-\\u00ff]*[\\u00e0-\\u00ff]*[\\u00e0-\\u00ff]*[\\u00e0-\\u00ff]*[\\u00e0-\\u00ff]*[\\u00e0-\\u00ff]*[\\u00e0-\\u00ff]*[\\u00e0-\\u00ff]*[\\u00e0-\\u00ff]*[\\u00e0-\\u00ff]*!'],
    62	    // iter-4 I4: escaped-metacharacter and control-char literals — the escape
    63	    // decoder dropped these, so the fills never triggered the backtrack.
    64	    ['escaped-metacharacter chained star', '\\(*\\(*\\(*\\(*\\(*\\(*\\(*\\(*\\(*\\(*\\(*\\(*!'],
    65	    ['newline-literal chained star', '\\n*\\n*\\n*\\n*\\n*\\n*\\n*\\n*\\n*\\n*\\n*\\n*!'],
    66	    ['dot chained star', '.*.*.*.*.*.*.*.*.*.*.*.*\\n'],
    67	  ];
    68	  for (const [label, pattern] of HANGS) {
    69	    it(`probeRegexSafe rejects: ${label}`, () => {
    70	      const started = Date.now();
    71	      const r = probeRegexSafe(pattern, '');
    72	      assert.equal(r.safe, false, `${label} must be caught by the probe`);
    73	      assert.ok(Date.now() - started < 10_000, 'the probe itself must return in bounded time');
    74	    });
    75	    it(`loadSignatures rejects the overlay: ${label}`, () => {
    76	      const started = Date.now();
    77	      assert.throws(() => loadWith({ matcher: { kind: 'regex', pattern } }), /ov/);
    78	      assert.ok(Date.now() - started < 10_000, 'rejection is bounded, not a hang');
    79	    });
    80	  }
    81	
    82	  it('still accepts ordinary linear patterns (no false positive)', () => {
    83	    assert.equal(probeRegexSafe('\\d+ requests remaining', '').safe, true);
    84	    assert.equal(probeRegexSafe('quota exceeded|usage limit', 'i').safe, true);
    85	  });
    86	});

    88	describe('structural rejection of chained overlapping quantified ATOMS (iter-4 I4)', () => {
    89	  // The structural scanner rejects these at LOAD — no probe timeout needed —
    90	  // because a finite probe alphabet cannot guarantee a non-matching tail for
    91	  // every atom (a `.` accepts almost everything).
    92	  const REJECTED = [
    93	    ['dot chain', '.*.*.*x'],
    94	    ['digit chain', '\\d*\\d*\\d*x'],
    95	    ['escaped-paren chain', '\\(*\\(*x'],
    96	    ['newline-literal chain', '\\n*\\n*x'],
    97	    ['class chain', '[a-z]*[a-z]*X'],
    98	    ['plus chain', '\\w+\\w+x'],
    99	    ['open-brace chain', 'a{2,}a{2,}x'],
   100	  ];
   101	  for (const [label, pattern] of REJECTED) {
   102	    it(`loadSignatures rejects a chained overlapping quantifier: ${label}`, () => {
   103	      const started = Date.now();
   104	      // The message must be the STRUCTURAL one — NOT the probe-timeout message —
   105	      // so this test pins the load-time scanner, not the 1000ms probe backstop
   106	      // (which would still "reject" a reverted scanner, just slowly).
   107	      assert.throws(() => loadWith({ matcher: { kind: 'regex', pattern } }), /chained overlapping unbounded quantifiers|backtrack super-linearly/);
   108	      assert.ok(Date.now() - started < 500, 'rejection is structural + immediate, well under the 1000ms probe deadline (proves the scanner, not the probe, rejected)');
   109	    });
   110	  }
   111	
   112	  const ACCEPTED = [
   113	    ['single quantified atom', "You've hit your \\S+ limit"],
   114	    ['disjoint chained stars', '\\d*\\s*!'],
   115	    ['disjoint literals', 'a+b+c+'],
   116	    ['alternation, no quantifier chain', 'usage limit exceeded|quota exceeded'],
   117	    ['quantified group over a distinct atom', '(abc)+\\d+'],
   118	    ['bounded repeats', '\\d{2,4}\\d{1,3}'],
   119	  ];
   120	  for (const [label, pattern] of ACCEPTED) {
   121	    it(`loadSignatures ACCEPTS a benign pattern (no false positive): ${label}`, () => {
   122	      const t = loadWith({ matcher: { kind: 'regex', pattern, flags: 'i' } });
   123	      assert.ok(t.signatures.some((s) => s.id === 'ov'), `${label} must load`);
   124	    });
   125	  }

    19	// Compatibility policy (plan §Compatibility; gate-2 major 13): documented
    20	// floors are ENFORCED for codex/cursor (below-floor = hooks unavailable →
    21	// protocol-only Tier C); the support claim is CAPPED at the versions this
    22	// build was verified against — newer gets an info line, never a failure.
    23	/** @type {Record<string, string>} */
    24	const VERSION_BINS = { 'claude-code': 'claude', codex: 'codex', cursor: 'cursor-agent' };
    25	/** @type {Record<string, {floor: string | null, label: string | null}>} */
    26	const FLOORS = {
    27	  'claude-code': { floor: '2.1', label: '2.1' }, // StopFailure hook documented on the 2.1 line (task-0 snapshot)
    28	  codex: { floor: '0.144', label: '0.144' }, // lifecycle hooks landed in 0.144
    29	  cursor: { floor: '2026.5', label: '2026.05' },
    30	};
    31	/** @type {Record<string, string>} */
    32	const TESTED = { 'claude-code': '2.1.201', codex: '0.144.1', cursor: '2026.05.01' };

    34	/** @param {any} text @returns {string | null} */
    35	const parseVersion = (text) => String(text).match(/\d+(?:\.\d+)+/)?.[0] ?? null;
    36	
    37	/** Numeric segment-wise version compare: negative when a < b. */
    38	function cmpVersions(/** @type {string} */ a, /** @type {string} */ b) {
    39	  const as = a.split('.').map(Number);
    40	  const bs = b.split('.').map(Number);
    41	  for (let i = 0; i < Math.max(as.length, bs.length); i++) {
    42	    const d = (as[i] ?? 0) - (bs[i] ?? 0);
    43	    if (d !== 0) return d < 0 ? -1 : 1;
    44	  }
    45	  return 0;
    46	}
    47	
    48	/** @param {string} platform @param {any} io @returns {Promise<string | null>} */
    49	async function versionOf(platform, io) {
    50	  try {
    51	    const r = await io.execFile(VERSION_BINS[platform], ['--version'], { timeout: PROBE_TIMEOUT_MS });
    52	    return parseVersion(`${r.stdout ?? ''} ${r.stderr ?? ''}`);
    53	  } catch {
    54	    return null;
    55	  }
    56	}
    57	
    58	/** @param {string} platform @param {string | null} v @returns {{id: string, ok: boolean, detail: string}} */
    59	function versionCheck(platform, v) {
    60	  const id = `version-${platform}`;
    61	  if (v === null) {
    62	    return { id, ok: true, detail: `${VERSION_BINS[platform]} version unverifiable (not installed or --version unsupported) — floor not enforced` };
    63	  }
    64	  const f = FLOORS[platform];
    65	  if (f.floor && cmpVersions(v, f.floor) < 0) {
    66	    return { id, ok: false, detail: `${platform} ${v} is below the supported floor ${f.label} — lifecycle hooks unavailable; the adapter degrades to protocol-only (Tier C)` };
    67	  }
    68	  if (cmpVersions(v, TESTED[platform]) > 0) {
    69	    return { id, ok: true, detail: `${platform} ${v} is newer than tested (${TESTED[platform]}) — untested; signature drift is absorbed by the overlay table` };
    70	  }
    71	  return { id, ok: true, detail: `${platform} ${v} (tested ${TESTED[platform]})` };
    72	}
    73	
    74	/**
    75	 * Codex/Cursor hook surface: installed / trusted / observed-executing. Trust
    76	 * is not verifiable from outside the harness, so the fidelity claim is gated
    77	 * on the CANARY — journal evidence that a hook of this platform actually ran.
    78	 * @param {any} io @param {string} root @param {any} p bundlePaths
    79	 * @param {string} platform @param {string} relPath @param {string} trustNote
    80	 */
    81	function hookSurfaceCheck(io, root, p, platform, relPath, trustNote) {
    82	  const id = `${platform}-hooks`;
    83	  let text = null;
    84	  try {
    85	    text = io.fs.readFileSync(`${root}/${relPath}`, 'utf8');
    86	  } catch {
    87	    text = null;
    88	  }
    89	  const installed = text !== null && text.includes('baton');
    90	  if (!installed) {
    91	    // Four explicit per-hook states (iter-4 I5 / plan §Codex,§Cursor adapters):
    92	    // installed / enabled / trusted / observed-executing. When nothing is
    93	    // installed every downstream state is false.
    94	    return {
    95	      id,
    96	      ok: true,
    97	      states: { installed: false, enabled: false, trusted: 'unknown', observed: false },
    98	      detail: `not installed — run 'baton init --${platform}' to write ${relPath}`,
    99	    };
   100	  }
   101	  let observed = false;
   102	  try {
   103	    for (const e of readAllTolerant(io.fs, p.journal).entries) {
   104	      if (e?.source === platform || String(e?.writerId ?? '').startsWith(`${platform}-`)) {
   105	        observed = true;
   106	        break;
   107	      }
   108	    }
   109	  } catch {
   110	    observed = false;
   111	  }
   112	  // `trusted` is NOT externally verifiable — Codex records trust against a hook
   113	  // HASH and Cursor gates on workspace trust, neither readable from here — so it
   114	  // is reported 'unknown' and the fidelity claim is gated on the observed canary.
   115	  // `enabled` follows installation for these harnesses (no separate enable step);
   116	  // the hook is only truly ACTIVE once trusted AND observed.
   117	  const states = { installed: true, enabled: true, trusted: 'unknown', observed };
   118	  return {
   119	    id,
   120	    ok: true,
   121	    states,
   122	    detail: observed
   123	      ? 'installed, enabled; trusted=unknown (not externally verifiable); execution OBSERVED (canary) — the ≤1-turn mechanical-staleness claim holds'
   124	      : `installed, enabled; trusted=unknown — ${trustNote}; execution not yet observed — the fidelity claim is gated on this canary`,
   125	  };
   126	}
   127	
   128	// Noninteractive status probes per platform (docs/design/platform-notes.md CLIs).
   129	// capOnSuccess caps what a clean exit PROVES (gate-2 iter-2 M3): `claude
   130	// --version` proves the binary is INSTALLED, not that the server was reached or
   131	// that auth is valid, so its capability is capped at "installed". The codex/
   132	// cursor probes are auth-checking status commands, so a clean exit legitimately
   133	// proves "reachable".
   134	const PROBES = [
   135	  { platform: 'claude-code', bin: 'claude', args: ['--version'], capOnSuccess: 'installed' },
   136	  { platform: 'codex', bin: 'codex', args: ['login', 'status'], capOnSuccess: 'reachable' },
   137	  { platform: 'cursor', bin: 'cursor-agent', args: ['status'], capOnSuccess: 'reachable' },
   138	];
   139	
   140	// Word-boundary-safe AI-attribution trailer patterns (plan §Attribution guard).
   141	const TRAILER_PATTERNS = [
   142	  /co-authored-by:[^\n]*\b(claude|gpt|copilot|codex)\b/i,
   143	  /generated (with|by)[^\n]*\bclaude\b/i,
   144	  /\u{1F916}/u,
   145	];
   146	
   147	/** @param {any} io @param {string} path @returns {string | null} */
   148	function readOrNull(io, path) {
   149	  try {
   150	    return io.fs.readFileSync(path, 'utf8');
   151	  } catch {
   152	    return null;
   153	  }
   154	}
   155	
   156	/**
   157	 * Classify one probe invocation per the two-dimension record: capability is
   158	 * the ordered ladder verified as far as the evidence goes; outcome is what the
   159	 * probe itself did. A server-issued limit response proves authentication.
   160	 * @param {{platform: string, bin: string, args: string[], capOnSuccess?: string}} probe @param {any} io @param {any} table
   161	 */
   162	async function runProbe(probe, io, table) {
   163	  try {
   164	    await io.execFile(probe.bin, probe.args, { timeout: PROBE_TIMEOUT_MS });
   165	    return { platform: probe.platform, capability: probe.capOnSuccess ?? 'reachable', outcome: 'ok' };
   166	  } catch (err) {
   167	    const e = /** @type {any} */ (err);
   168	    if (e?.code === 'ENOENT') return { platform: probe.platform, capability: null, outcome: 'error' };
   169	    if (e?.killed === true) return { platform: probe.platform, capability: 'installed', outcome: 'timeout' };
   170	    const text = `${e?.stdout ?? ''}\n${e?.stderr ?? ''}`;
   171	    if (table && classify({ text, exitCode: 1, platform: probe.platform, table, structured: null }).class === 'usage-limit') {
   172	      return { platform: probe.platform, capability: 'authenticated', outcome: 'rate-limited' };
   173	    }
   174	    if (/authenticated as|logged in as/i.test(text) && /unreachable|ECONN|offline|network/i.test(text)) {
   175	      return { platform: probe.platform, capability: 'authenticated', outcome: 'error' };
   176	    }
   177	    if (/not logged in|log ?in first|unauthorized|authenticat/i.test(text)) {
   178	      return { platform: probe.platform, capability: 'installed', outcome: 'error' };
   179	    }
   180	    return { platform: probe.platform, capability: 'installed', outcome: 'error' };
   181	  }
   182	}
   183	
   184	/** @param {any} io @param {string} root @returns {Promise<{id: string, ok: boolean, detail?: string}>} */
   185	async function gitGuardCheck(io, root) {
   186	  let log;
   187	  try {
   188	    log = await io.execFile('git', ['log', '-n', String(GUARD_COMMIT_BOUND), '--format=%h %s%n%n%b'], { cwd: root, timeout: 5000 });
   189	  } catch {
   190	    return { id: 'git-guard', ok: true, detail: 'git unavailable — commit scan skipped' };
   191	  }
   192	  // Chunks start at a line beginning with an abbreviated sha + space.
   193	  const chunks = log.stdout.split(/^(?=[0-9a-f]{7,40} )/m).filter((/** @type {string} */ c) => c.trim() !== '');
   194	  for (const chunk of chunks) {
   195	    if (TRAILER_PATTERNS.some((re) => re.test(chunk))) {
   196	      const sha = chunk.slice(0, chunk.indexOf(' '));
   197	      return {
   198	        id: 'git-guard',
   199	        ok: false,
   200	        detail: `commit ${sha} carries an AI attribution trailer — rewrite it before publishing (git rebase -i / git commit --amend)`,
   201	      };
   202	    }
   203	  }
   204	  return { id: 'git-guard', ok: true, detail: `no AI trailers in the last ${GUARD_COMMIT_BOUND} commits` };
   205	}
   206	
   207	/**
   208	 * `baton doctor` — machine health report: attribution settings, git-guard
   209	 * trailer scan, journal size, filesystem boundary, and per-platform probe
   210	 * records ({capability, outcome}, 3 s bound, 15-min cache). Report-only by
   211	 * default; `--strict` turns any failing check into exit 1.
   212	 * @param {string[]} args @param {any} io
   213	 * @returns {Promise<number>}
   214	 */
   215	export async function cmdDoctor(args, io) {
   216	  const { flags } = parseFlags(args);
   217	  const root = resolveRoot(io, flags);
   218	  const p = bundlePaths(root);
   219	  /** @type {{id: string, ok: boolean, detail?: string}[]} */
   220	  const checks = [];
   221	
   222	  // Attribution settings: repo-level first, then the user's global file.
   223	  const settingsText = readOrNull(io, `${root}/.claude/settings.json`) ?? readOrNull(io, `${io.env?.HOME ?? ''}/.claude/settings.json`);
   224	  let attrOk = false;
   225	  try {
   226	    const s = settingsText === null ? null : JSON.parse(settingsText);
   227	    attrOk = s?.attribution?.commit === '' && s?.attribution?.pr === '';
   228	  } catch {
   229	    attrOk = false;
   230	  }
   231	  checks.push({
   232	    id: 'attribution-settings',
   233	    ok: attrOk,
   234	    detail: attrOk ? 'attribution.commit and attribution.pr are ""' : 'set {"attribution":{"commit":"","pr":""}} in .claude/settings.json (baton init does this)',
   235	  });
   236	
   237	  checks.push(await gitGuardCheck(io, root));
   238	
   239	  const journalText = readOrNull(io, p.journal);
   240	  const journalBytes = journalText === null ? 0 : Buffer.byteLength(journalText);
   241	  checks.push({
   242	    id: 'journal-size',
   243	    ok: journalBytes <= JOURNAL_WARN_BYTES,
   244	    detail: journalBytes > JOURNAL_WARN_BYTES ? `journal is ${(journalBytes / 1048576).toFixed(1)} MB (> 5 MB) — finalize to rotate it` : `${journalBytes} bytes`,
   245	  });
   246	
   247	
   248	  // Platform probes + version probes + mount detection: every non-git child
   249	  // invocation rides the same 15-min cache in the state dir.
   250	  const cachePath = `${p.logDir}/probe-cache.json`;
   251	  const cached = safeReadJson(io.fs, cachePath);
   252	  /** @type {any[]} */
   253	  let platforms;
   254	  /** @type {Record<string, string | null>} */
   255	  let versions;
   256	  /** @type {string | null} */
   257	  let mountSource;
   258	  if (cached.ok && typeof cached.value?.at === 'string' && Date.parse(io.now()) - Date.parse(cached.value.at) < PROBE_CACHE_MS && Array.isArray(cached.value.records)) {
   259	    platforms = cached.value.records;
   260	    versions = cached.value.versions ?? {};
   261	    mountSource = cached.value.mount ?? null;
   262	  } else {
   263	    /** @type {any} */
   264	    let table = null;
   265	    try {
   266	      // Packaged data, not repo state: read through the real fs so probes can
   267	      // classify limit responses regardless of the injected io.fs contents.
   268	      table = loadSignatures({ builtinPath: BUILTIN_SIGNATURES }, { fs: realFs });
   269	    } catch {
   270	      table = null;
   271	    }
   272	    platforms = [];
   273	    for (const probe of PROBES) platforms.push(await runProbe(probe, io, table));
   274	    versions = {};
   275	    for (const platform of Object.keys(VERSION_BINS)) versions[platform] = await versionOf(platform, io);
   276	    mountSource = null;
   277	    try {
   278	      const df = await io.execFile('df', ['-P', root], { timeout: 2000 });
   279	      mountSource = ((df.stdout ?? '').split('\n')[1] ?? '').trim().split(/\s+/)[0] || null;
   280	    } catch {
   281	      mountSource = null;
   282	    }
   283	    // Jail before writing the probe cache (iter-3 F4): the cache rides
   284	    // .handoff/log, so a symlinked .handoff or log dir must never redirect the
   285	    // write outside the repository (threat model). Diagnostics are best-effort —
   286	    // on an unsafe tree we simply skip caching; the atomic write already
   287	    // prevents a torn cache under concurrent doctors.
   288	    if (checkHandoffTree(root, io).ok) {
   289	      ensureDir(io.fs, p.logDir);
   290	      atomicWriteJson(io.fs, cachePath, { at: io.now(), records: platforms, versions, mount: mountSource });
   291	    }
   292	  }
   293	
   294	  // Network-filesystem detection (gate-2 major 13): a mount source that looks
   295	  // like SMB/NFS fails the check; unverifiable degrades to the static boundary.
   296	  if (mountSource === null) {
   297	    checks.push({
   298	      id: 'filesystem',
   299	      ok: true,
   300	      detail: 'mount type unverifiable — v1 supports local filesystems only; lock/rename atomicity is not guaranteed on network or cloud-sync mounts',
     1	import { describe, it } from 'node:test';
     2	import assert from 'node:assert/strict';
     3	import { makeIo } from '../helpers/fakeio.mjs';
     4	import { cmdDoctor } from '../../core/src/commands/doctor.mjs';
     5	import { bundlePaths } from '../../core/src/bundle/store.mjs';
     6	
     7	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
     8	// Command-level contract for core/src/commands/doctor.mjs. Direct import over a
     9	// fakeio whose execFile is REPLACED with a responder (doctor probes platforms
    10	// and scans git via the async injected execFile, so cmdDoctor is async).
    11	//
    12	// TARGET MODULE: core/src/commands/doctor.mjs (sole target — its absence is the
    13	// only reason this file is RED). doctor internally consumes git/guard.mjs,
    14	// git/snapshot.mjs and the probe layer; this file imports ONLY doctor and drives
    15	// everything through it (git/guard.mjs is NOT a Wave-D target, so it is exercised
    16	// through doctor, never imported here).
    17	//
    18	// Source of truth: plan §Role matrix "Availability probing" (doctor probes each
    19	// platform noninteractively, HARD 3s bound per probe, results cached 15 min;
    20	// probe record = two SEPARATE dimensions: capability {installed→authenticated→
    21	// reachable} recorded as far as verifiable, and outcome {ok|rate-limited|error|
    22	// timeout}); §Attribution guard layer 2 (doctor scans the LAST 50 COMMITS for AI
    23	// co-author/generated-with trailers, WORD-BOUNDARY-safe, and flags with
    24	// remediation); §"Journal rotation" (doctor warns at journal > 5 MB);
    25	// §"Filesystem support boundary" (doctor detects + warns on network/shared
    26	// mounts); §CLI contract (--strict exit).
    27	//
    28	// PINS (where the spec left the command surface open — the implementer conforms):
    29	//   D1. SIGNATURE: cmdDoctor(args, io) -> Promise<number>. --json emits exactly
    30	//       one compact {ok,data,warnings,error} envelope on stdout.
    31	//   D2. --json data carries CHECKS and per-platform PROBE RECORDS. Shape is read
    32	//       tolerantly (array of {id, ok, detail?} OR a map for checks; array of
    33	//       {platform, capability, outcome, ...} OR a map for platforms), so the
    34	//       exact container is not over-pinned; what IS pinned:
    35	//         - a check whose id matches /attribution/ reflects the .claude/
    36	//           settings.json attribution state (ok when commit==="" && pr==="");
    37	//         - a check whose id matches /guard|commit|trailer/ is the AI-trailer
    38	//           commit scan;
    39	//         - a check whose id matches /journal/ warns when the journal > 5 MB;
    40	//         - a check whose id matches /filesystem|network|mount|fs/ exists;
    41	//         - EVERY platform record exposes BOTH dimensions: `capability` ∈
    42	//           {installed, authenticated, reachable} (the ordered cumulative
    43	//           ladder; verifier fold F5) and `outcome` ∈ {ok, rate-limited, error,
    44	//           timeout}. A not-installed platform may fall below the ladder; every
    45	//           INSTALLED platform's capability must be one of the three rungs.
    46	//   D3. PROBES ARE TIME-BOUNDED: every probe (non-git) execFile call is invoked
    47	//       with an options object carrying timeout <= 3000 (the hard 3 s bound),
    48	//       mirroring git/snapshot.mjs's timeout:5000 convention.
    49	//   D4. PROBES ARE CACHED ~15 min: a SECOND doctor run within the window makes
    50	//       ZERO new probe (non-git) execFile calls (cache reuse via io.fs), while a
    51	//       run AFTER the window re-probes. (The cache lives under io.fs so it
    52	//       persists across calls; its exact path is left to the implementer.)
    53	//   D5. GIT-GUARD is WORD-BOUNDARY-safe: a commit carrying a "Co-Authored-By:
    54	//       Claude" trailer is flagged (its sha surfaces in the envelope); a history
    55	//       whose only "Claude"-ish token is the name "Claudette Smith" is NOT
    56	//       flagged. doctor scans FULL commit messages (subject + body), where
    57	//       trailers live, and (verifier fold F6) the scan is BOUNDED to the last 50
    58	//       commits — the git log invocation must carry `-n 50` / `-n50` /
    59	//       `--max-count=50` / `--max-count 50` (any of these forms).
    60	//   D6. --strict EXIT: without --strict doctor is report-only (exit 0 even with
    61	//       findings); with --strict a failing check (e.g. a flagged AI trailer)
    62	//       exits non-zero (1). An all-green machine exits 0 either way.
    63	//   D7. PROBE STAGING (verifier fold F5 — the fixture oracle, pinned here since
    64	//       the plan names the dimensions but not the probe wire protocol):
    65	//       doctor probes each platform by invoking its CLI binary noninteractively
    66	//       via io.execFile — `claude` for claude-code, `codex` for codex,
    67	//       `cursor-agent` for cursor (the local CLIs named in docs/design/
    68	//       platform-notes.md; multiple calls per binary are permitted, all answered
    69	//       identically by the fixture). The probe result is classified from the
    70	//       invocation result:
    71	//         - clean resolution                        -> capability 'reachable', outcome 'ok'
    72	//         - output matching /not logged in|log ?in first|unauthorized|authenticat/i
    73	//                                                   -> capability 'installed' (auth
    74	//                                                      unverifiable), outcome != 'ok'
    75	//         - output matching the platform's limit signatures (signatures.v1.json,
    76	//           e.g. "You've hit your usage limit")     -> outcome 'rate-limited'
    77	//           (a server-issued limit response also PROVES auth: capability is at
    78	//           least 'authenticated')
    79	//         - output with auth evidence (/authenticated as|logged in as/i) plus a
    80	//           network failure (/unreachable|ECONN|offline|network/i)
    81	//                                                   -> capability 'authenticated',
    82	//                                                      outcome 'error'
    83	//         - a rejection shaped like an execFile timeout kill (err.killed === true)
    84	//                                                   -> outcome 'timeout'
    85	//         - any other rejection                     -> outcome 'error'
    86	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    87	
    88	const ROOT = '/repo';
    89	const paths = bundlePaths(ROOT);
    90	const NOW = '2026-07-11T00:00:00.000Z';
    91	const plus = (base, ms) => new Date(Date.parse(base) + ms).toISOString();
    92	
    93	const ATTR_OK = JSON.stringify({ attribution: { commit: '', pr: '' } }, null, 2);
    94	
    95	// A git log whose parser sees full commit messages. One commit carries a real
    96	// AI co-author trailer (its sha must be flagged); the rest are clean.
    97	const GIT_LOG_DIRTY =
    98	  'a1b2c3d4 Implement the parser\n\nCo-Authored-By: Claude <noreply@anthropic.com>\n\n' +
    99	  'c4d5e6f7 Refactor utils\n\nA routine change with no trailer.\n';
   100	
   101	// Word-boundary control: "Claudette Smith" contains "Claude" as a prefix but is
   102	// NOT an AI-attribution trailer — a \bClaude\b-style scan must NOT flag it.
   103	const GIT_LOG_CLAUDETTE =
   104	  'b2c3d4e5 Add greeting helper\n\nThanks to Claudette Smith for the bug report.\n\n' +
   105	  'e5f6a7b8 Tidy tests\n\nNo attribution here.\n';
   106	
   107	const GIT_LOG_CLEAN = 'f0f0f0f0 Initial commit\n\nClean history.\n';
   108	
   109	// The pinned platform -> probe binary mapping (D7).
   110	const BIN_FOR = { 'claude-code': 'claude', codex: 'codex', cursor: 'cursor-agent' };
   111	
   112	/** Answer one probe invocation according to its staged behavior kind (D7). */
   113	function respondProbe(kind, resolve, reject) {
   114	  switch (kind) {
   115	    case 'ok':
   116	      return resolve({ stdout: 'ok\n', stderr: '' });
   117	    case 'auth-fail': {
   118	      const e = new Error('Command failed');
   119	      e.code = 1;
   120	      e.stdout = '';
   121	      e.stderr = 'Error: not logged in — run the login flow first\n';
   122	      return reject(e);
   123	    }
   124	    case 'net-fail': {
   125	      const e = new Error('Command failed');
   126	      e.code = 1;
   127	      e.stdout = '';
   128	      e.stderr = 'Authenticated as user@example.test, but the service is unreachable (ECONNREFUSED)\n';
   129	      return reject(e);
   130	    }
   131	    case 'rate-limited': {
   132	      const e = new Error('Command failed');
   133	      e.code = 1;
   134	      e.stdout = '';
   135	      e.stderr = "You've hit your usage limit. Please try again at 3pm.\n";
   136	      return reject(e);
   137	    }
   138	    case 'timeout': {
   139	      const e = new Error('timed out');
   140	      e.killed = true;
   141	      e.signal = 'SIGTERM';
   142	      return reject(e);
   143	    }
   144	    case 'error': {
   145	      const e = new Error('Command failed');
   146	      e.code = 1;
   147	      e.stdout = '';
   148	      e.stderr = 'fatal: unexpected internal error\n';
   149	      return reject(e);
   150	    }
   151	    default:
   152	      return resolve({ stdout: 'ok\n', stderr: '' });
   153	  }
   154	}
   155	
   156	/**
   157	 * Build a doctor io: fakeio for fs/stdout, but execFile REPLACED with a
   158	 * responder that answers git subcommands from `gitLog`/clean fixtures and
   159	 * treats every non-git command as a platform probe answered per `probes`
   160	 * (a map of binary basename -> behavior kind; default all 'ok').
   161	 * io.__calls records {cmd, args, opts}.
   162	 */
   163	function makeDoctorIo({ files = {}, gitLog = GIT_LOG_CLEAN, now = NOW, probes = {} } = {}) {
   164	  const io = makeIo({ files, env: { HOME: '/home/user' }, now });
   165	  const calls = [];
   166	  io.__calls = calls;
   167	  io.execFile = (cmd, args = [], opts = {}) => {
   168	    calls.push({ cmd, args: [...args], opts });
   169	    return new Promise((resolve, reject) => {
   170	      if (cmd === 'git') {
   171	        const sub = args[0];
   172	        if (sub === 'log') return resolve({ stdout: gitLog, stderr: '' });
   173	        if (sub === 'rev-parse') return resolve({ stdout: args.includes('HEAD') && !args.includes('--abbrev-ref') ? 'f0f0f0f0\n' : 'main\n', stderr: '' });
   174	        return resolve({ stdout: '', stderr: '' });
   175	      }
   176	      // Non-git command === a platform probe; behavior keyed on the binary basename.
   177	      const bin = String(cmd).split('/').pop();
   178	      return respondProbe(probes[bin] ?? 'ok', resolve, reject);
   179	    });
   180	  };
   181	  return io;
   182	}
   183	
   184	const probeCalls = (io) => io.__calls.filter((c) => c.cmd !== 'git');
   185	
   186	/** Parse the single compact --json envelope and check its wire shape. */
   187	function envelope(io) {
   188	  const parsed = JSON.parse(io.stdoutText());
   189	  assert.equal(io.stdoutText(), JSON.stringify(parsed) + '\n', 'stdout is exactly one compact envelope + newline');
   190	  assert.ok('ok' in parsed && 'data' in parsed && 'warnings' in parsed && 'error' in parsed, 'envelope carries ok/data/warnings/error');
   191	  return parsed;
   192	}
   193	
   194	/** Normalize data.checks (array of {id,ok} OR a map) to an array of {id, ok, ...}. */
   195	function checkList(env) {
   196	  const c = env.data && env.data.checks;
   197	  if (Array.isArray(c)) return c;
   198	  if (c && typeof c === 'object') return Object.entries(c).map(([id, v]) => ({ id, ...(v && typeof v === 'object' ? v : { ok: v }) }));
   199	  return [];
   200	}
   201	const findCheck = (env, re) => checkList(env).find((c) => re.test(String(c.id)));
   202	
   203	/** Normalize data.platforms (array OR map) to an array of records. */
   204	function platformRecords(env) {
   205	  const p = env.data && env.data.platforms;
   206	  if (Array.isArray(p)) return p;
   207	  if (p && typeof p === 'object') return Object.entries(p).map(([platform, v]) => ({ platform, ...(v && typeof v === 'object' ? v : {}) }));
   208	  return [];
   209	}
   210	const recordFor = (env, platform) => platformRecords(env).find((r) => r.platform === platform);
   211	
   212	const LADDER = ['installed', 'authenticated', 'reachable'];
   213	const OUTCOMES = ['ok', 'rate-limited', 'error', 'timeout'];
   214	
   215	const ATTR_FILES = { [`${ROOT}/.claude/settings.json`]: ATTR_OK, ['/home/user/.claude/settings.json']: ATTR_OK };
   216	
   217	// ===========================================================================
   218	describe('doctor — envelope + check aggregation', () => {
   219	  it('--json emits one compact envelope carrying checks and per-platform probe records', async () => {
   220	    const io = makeDoctorIo({ files: { ...ATTR_FILES } });
   221	    const code = await cmdDoctor(['--json'], io);
   222	    assert.equal(code, 0, `an all-green machine exits 0; stderr: ${io.stderrText()}`);
   223	
   224	    const env = envelope(io);
   225	    assert.ok(checkList(env).length > 0, 'doctor reports a set of checks');
   226	
   227	    assert.ok(findCheck(env, /attribution/), 'an attribution-settings check is present');
   228	    assert.ok(findCheck(env, /guard|commit|trailer/), 'a git-guard commit-scan check is present');
   229	    assert.ok(findCheck(env, /journal/), 'a journal-size check is present');
   230	    assert.ok(findCheck(env, /filesystem|network|mount|fs/), 'a filesystem check is present');
   231	  });
   232	
   233	  it('reports the four per-hook states distinctly: installed/enabled/trusted/observed (iter-4 I5)', async () => {
   234	    // Not installed → every state false.
   235	    const io0 = makeDoctorIo({ files: { ...ATTR_FILES } });
   236	    await cmdDoctor(['--json'], io0);
   237	    const s0 = findCheck(envelope(io0), /codex-hooks/).states;
   238	    assert.deepEqual(s0, { installed: false, enabled: false, trusted: 'unknown', observed: false });
   239	
   240	    // Installed (hooks.json references baton), no canary yet → trusted unknown, observed false.
   241	    const io1 = makeDoctorIo({ files: { ...ATTR_FILES, [`${ROOT}/.codex/hooks.json`]: '{"hooks":{"Stop":"node baton.mjs checkpoint"}}' } });
   242	    await cmdDoctor(['--json'], io1);
   243	    const s1 = findCheck(envelope(io1), /codex-hooks/).states;
   244	    assert.equal(s1.installed, true);
   245	    assert.equal(s1.enabled, true);
   246	    assert.equal(s1.trusted, 'unknown', 'trust is not externally verifiable');
   247	    assert.equal(s1.observed, false, 'no canary yet');
   248	
   249	    // Installed + a journal entry from codex → observed true (fidelity canary).
   250	    const journal = JSON.stringify({ seq: 1, ts: NOW, type: 'note', source: 'codex', payload: { text: 'ran' } }) + '\n';
   251	    const io2 = makeDoctorIo({ files: { ...ATTR_FILES, [`${ROOT}/.codex/hooks.json`]: '{"hooks":{"Stop":"node baton.mjs checkpoint"}}', [`${ROOT}/.handoff/journal.ndjson`]: journal } });
   252	    await cmdDoctor(['--json'], io2);
   253	    const s2 = findCheck(envelope(io2), /codex-hooks/).states;
   254	    assert.equal(s2.observed, true, 'a codex-sourced journal entry is the observed canary');
   255	  });
   256	
   257	  it('reports the four states for the cursor hook surface too (iter-4 I5)', async () => {
   258	    // Same shared reporter, but pin cursor explicitly so a future divergence is caught.
   259	    const io = makeDoctorIo({ files: { ...ATTR_FILES, [`${ROOT}/.cursor/hooks.json`]: '{"version":1,"hooks":{"stop":"node baton.mjs checkpoint"}}' } });
   260	    await cmdDoctor(['--json'], io);
   261	    const s = findCheck(envelope(io), /cursor-hooks/).states;
   262	    assert.equal(s.installed, true);
   263	    assert.equal(s.enabled, true);
   264	    assert.equal(s.trusted, 'unknown');
   265	    assert.equal(s.observed, false);
   266	  });
   267	});
   268	
   269	// ===========================================================================
   270	describe('doctor — per-platform probe records expose BOTH dimensions (F5)', () => {
   271	  it('with every probe healthy: outcome is "ok"; capability reflects what each probe PROVES (gate-2 iter-2 M3)', async () => {
   272	    const io = makeDoctorIo({ files: { ...ATTR_FILES } });
   273	    await cmdDoctor(['--json'], io);
   274	    const records = platformRecords(envelope(io));
   275	    assert.ok(records.length >= 3, 'the three platforms each get a probe record');
   276	    for (const r of records) {
   277	      assert.ok(LADDER.includes(r.capability), `${r.platform}: capability must be a ladder rung (${LADDER.join('→')}); got ${JSON.stringify(r.capability)}`);
   278	      assert.ok(OUTCOMES.includes(r.outcome), `${r.platform}: outcome must be one of ${OUTCOMES.join('|')}; got ${JSON.stringify(r.outcome)}`);
   279	      assert.equal(r.outcome, 'ok', `${r.platform}: a clean probe outcome is ok`);
   280	    }
   281	    const cap = Object.fromEntries(records.map((/** @type {any} */ r) => [r.platform, r.capability]));
   282	    // claude-code's probe is `claude --version` — a clean exit proves only that
   283	    // the binary is INSTALLED, not that the server was reached or auth is valid.
   284	    assert.equal(cap['claude-code'], 'installed', 'a --version probe proves installed, never reachable/authenticated');
   285	    // codex/cursor probes are auth-checking status commands → a clean exit reaches.
   286	    assert.equal(cap.codex, 'reachable', 'a clean auth-status probe verifies the full ladder');
   287	    assert.equal(cap.cursor, 'reachable', 'a clean auth-status probe verifies the full ladder');
   288	  });
   289	
   290	  it('probe fixtures A: installed-only (auth failure), rate-limited (proves auth), reachable (clean)', async () => {
   291	    const io = makeDoctorIo({
   292	      files: { ...ATTR_FILES },
   293	      probes: { [BIN_FOR['claude-code']]: 'auth-fail', [BIN_FOR.codex]: 'rate-limited', [BIN_FOR.cursor]: 'ok' },
   294	    });
   295	    await cmdDoctor(['--json'], io);
   296	    const env = envelope(io);
   297	
   298	    const cc = recordFor(env, 'claude-code');
   299	    assert.equal(cc.capability, 'installed', 'an auth failure stops the ladder at installed (auth unverifiable)');
   300	    assert.notEqual(cc.outcome, 'ok', 'an auth-failed probe is not outcome ok');
   301	    assert.ok(OUTCOMES.includes(cc.outcome));
   302	
   303	    const cx = recordFor(env, 'codex');
   304	    assert.equal(cx.outcome, 'rate-limited', 'a server-issued limit response records outcome rate-limited');
   305	    assert.ok(['authenticated', 'reachable'].includes(cx.capability), 'a rate-limit response proves at least authenticated');
   306	
   307	    const cu = recordFor(env, 'cursor');
   308	    assert.equal(cu.capability, 'reachable', 'a clean probe verifies reachable');
   309	    assert.equal(cu.outcome, 'ok');
   310	  });
   311	
   312	  it('probe fixtures B: authenticated-but-unreachable, timeout, error', async () => {
   313	    const io = makeDoctorIo({
   314	      files: { ...ATTR_FILES },
   315	      probes: { [BIN_FOR['claude-code']]: 'net-fail', [BIN_FOR.codex]: 'timeout', [BIN_FOR.cursor]: 'error' },
   316	    });
   317	    await cmdDoctor(['--json'], io);
   318	    const env = envelope(io);
   319	
   320	    const cc = recordFor(env, 'claude-code');
   321	    assert.equal(cc.capability, 'authenticated', 'auth evidence + a network failure stops the ladder at authenticated');
   322	    assert.equal(cc.outcome, 'error', 'the failed reach attempt is outcome error');
   323	
   324	    const cx = recordFor(env, 'codex');
   325	    assert.equal(cx.outcome, 'timeout', 'a killed (3s-bound) probe records outcome timeout');
   326	    assert.ok(LADDER.includes(cx.capability), 'capability is still recorded as far as verifiable');
   327	
   328	    const cu = recordFor(env, 'cursor');
   329	    assert.equal(cu.outcome, 'error', 'a generic probe failure records outcome error');
   330	    assert.ok(LADDER.includes(cu.capability));
   331	  });
   332	});
   333	
   334	// ===========================================================================
   335	describe('doctor — probes are time-bounded and cached', () => {
   336	  it('every probe execFile call carries a timeout <= 3000 (the hard 3s bound)', async () => {
   337	    const io = makeDoctorIo({ files: { ...ATTR_FILES } });
   338	    await cmdDoctor(['--json'], io);
   339	    const probes = probeCalls(io);
   340	    assert.ok(probes.length > 0, 'doctor actually probes the platforms');
   341	    for (const c of probes) {
   342	      assert.equal(typeof c.opts.timeout, 'number', `probe ${c.cmd} must pass a numeric timeout`);
   343	      assert.ok(c.opts.timeout <= 3000, `probe ${c.cmd} timeout must be <= 3000; got ${c.opts.timeout}`);
   344	    }
   345	  });
   346	
   347	  it('a second run within the cache window makes ZERO new probe calls; a run past 15 min re-probes', async () => {
   348	    const io = makeDoctorIo({ files: { ...ATTR_FILES } });
   349	
   350	    await cmdDoctor(['--json'], io);
   351	    const afterFirst = probeCalls(io).length;
   352	    assert.ok(afterFirst > 0, 'the first run probes');
   353	
   354	    // Second run, same clock (well within 15 min): must reuse the cache.
   355	    await cmdDoctor(['--json'], io);
   356	    assert.equal(probeCalls(io).length, afterFirst, 'a cached second run makes no new probe calls (15-min cache reuse)');
   357	
   358	    // Third run, clock advanced past the window: cache expired -> re-probe.
   359	    io.setNow(plus(NOW, 16 * 60 * 1000));
   360	    await cmdDoctor(['--json'], io);
   361	    assert.ok(probeCalls(io).length > afterFirst, 'a run past the 15-min window re-probes');
   362	  });
   363	});
   364	
   365	// ===========================================================================
   366	describe('doctor — attribution-settings check', () => {
   367	  it('passes when settings carry attribution.commit==="" and .pr===""', async () => {
   368	    const io = makeDoctorIo({ files: { ...ATTR_FILES } });
   369	    await cmdDoctor(['--json'], io);
   370	    assert.equal(findCheck(envelope(io), /attribution/).ok, true, 'attribution present -> check ok');
   371	  });
   372	
   373	  it('fails when the attribution keys are absent', async () => {
   374	    const noAttr = JSON.stringify({ theme: 'dark' });
   375	    const io = makeDoctorIo({ files: { [`${ROOT}/.claude/settings.json`]: noAttr, ['/home/user/.claude/settings.json']: noAttr } });
   376	    await cmdDoctor(['--json'], io);
   377	    assert.equal(findCheck(envelope(io), /attribution/).ok, false, 'missing attribution -> check fails with remediation');
   378	  });
   379	});
   380	
   381	// ===========================================================================
   382	describe('doctor — journal size warning', () => {
   383	  it('a journal > 5 MB flags the journal check; a small journal does not', async () => {
   384	    const big = 'x'.repeat(5 * 1024 * 1024 + 1) + '\n';
   385	    const ioBig = makeDoctorIo({ files: { ...ATTR_FILES, [paths.journal]: big } });
   386	    await cmdDoctor(['--json'], ioBig);
   387	    assert.equal(findCheck(envelope(ioBig), /journal/).ok, false, 'a journal over 5 MB warns');
   388	
   389	    const ioSmall = makeDoctorIo({ files: { ...ATTR_FILES, [paths.journal]: 'small\n' } });
   390	    await cmdDoctor(['--json'], ioSmall);
   391	    assert.equal(findCheck(envelope(ioSmall), /journal/).ok, true, 'a small journal is fine');
   392	  });
   393	});
   394

   396	describe('doctor — git-guard commit scan (word-boundary safe, bounded to 50)', () => {
   397	  it('flags a Co-Authored-By: Claude trailer, names the offending sha, and bounds the log to the last 50 commits (F6)', async () => {
   398	    const io = makeDoctorIo({ files: { ...ATTR_FILES }, gitLog: GIT_LOG_DIRTY });

   410	      joined,
   411	      /(?:^|\s)(?:-n ?50|--max-count[= ]50)(?:\s|$)/,
   412	      `git log must be bounded to 50 commits via -n 50 / -n50 / --max-count=50 / --max-count 50; got: ${joined}`,

   414	  });

`PostToolUse` rejected: per-tool-call spawns for incoherent mid-turn state; Stop gives the same worst-case staleness (one turn) at a fraction of the cost.

Commands: **`/baton:handoff`** (model-invocable so Claude can propose it near limits; writes the narrative — auditing every "done" claim against tool evidence — then seals via finalize, then prints the destination's resume command); **`/baton:receive`** (`disable-model-invocation: true`; runs receive → one AskUserQuestion with two questions: origin platform with detected option first, reason for switch → ack runs the core commit transaction (received seal archived, this session adopted as owner, fresh `open` generation; double-receive prevented by the one-time token; command tests assert the active/open + archived/received outcome) → evidence audit of bundle git snapshot vs live HEAD/status → announce role-remap table → auto-continue next step, pausing only per AGENTS.md boundaries); **`/baton:baton-setup`** (doctor + choose surfaces + init + template scaffold with diff preview). Receive-side continuation is a **skill** (`handoff-protocol`), not an agent — the main thread must inherit the task; a subagent would strand context. Optional v2 `handoff-verifier` agent (fresh-context bundle audit) is out of v1 scope.

### Codex adapter (Tier A− — hook-driven as of Codex >= 0.144)

Gate-1 correction: Codex now ships lifecycle hooks, so the adapter is redesigned around them. Surfaces: (1) **repo `.codex/hooks.json`** (written by `init --codex`, merged never clobbered): `Stop` → mechanical checkpoint, `PreCompact` → context-boundary checkpoint, `SessionStart` → pending-handoff notice via injected context. No StopFailure equivalent exists on Codex, so a limit death is bounded by the last Stop checkpoint (one turn worst case). (2) `.agents/skills/baton-handoff/SKILL.md` per the Agent Skills standard — carries the receive/handoff protocol, invoked as `$baton-handoff` or by description match. (3) Repo `AGENTS.md` Handoff Protocol section as the always-on instruction layer (narrative checkpoints after subtasks / before risky ops / at wind-down). (4) `~/.codex/prompts/*.md` demoted to **legacy fallback only** (deprecated upstream), written only with `--with-legacy-prompts`. The notify shim is **cut** — hooks make it obsolete; `~/.codex/config.toml` is never touched. **Trust gate** (verified, Gate-1 iteration 2): Codex requires per-definition review of non-managed command hooks — trust is recorded against the hook's hash, and new/changed hooks are skipped until re-trusted via `/hooks`. Setup and doctor therefore report four distinct states per hook — installed / enabled / **trusted** / observed-executing (canary event) — and the fidelity claim is gated on the canary, with setup guiding the user through `/hooks` when review is pending. Hook definitions include `commandWindows` overrides. Fidelity statement ships verbatim in README + doctor: mechanical staleness ≤ 1 turn via Stop hook **once trusted**; narrative staleness bounded by protocol compliance; git state always re-derived at receive. Requires codex-cli >= 0.144 (doctor enforces; older versions fall back to protocol-only Tier C with an explicit warning).

### Cursor adapter (Tier A− — hook-driven; event names verified against official docs, Gate-1 iteration 2)

Surfaces: `.cursor/hooks.json` (project-level, `{"version":1,"hooks":{...}}`) with the **verified** events — `stop` → mechanical checkpoint per agent turn (same semantics as Claude Code `Stop`); `afterFileEdit` → debounced checkpoint (debounce enforced by the CLI, one real checkpoint per 2 min); `beforeShellExecution` with a git-commit matcher → checkpoint before agent-issued commits (**documented limitation: commits made through the SCM UI are invisible to hooks — no pre-commit event exists**); `sessionStart` → pending-handoff notice; `preCompact` → context-boundary checkpoint. Plus `.cursor/commands/{receive,handoff,baton-setup}.md` (CLI loading unconfirmed — convenience layer; every behavior also reachable via AGENTS.md, which cursor-agent reads natively, and via the universal fallback **`baton receive --print-prompt`**) and a ≤5-line `.cursor/rules/baton-handoff.mdc` (`alwaysApply: true`). hooks.json written only after explicit confirmation, merged not clobbered; project hooks require a **trusted workspace** — doctor checks trust and gates the fidelity claim on an observed hook canary. Fidelity: mechanical staleness ≤ 1 turn via `stop` when trusted; untrusted/cloud contexts degrade to protocol-only with an explicit warning.

### Templates: ideal AGENTS.md + CLAUDE.md

Design rule resolving the two-model-family tension: **every shared invariant appears exactly once in AGENTS.md**; model-specific steering is quarantined (short Claude block in CLAUDE.md; scoped Codex notes only if ever needed). Budget: AGENTS.md ≤220 lines (handoff section ≤25), enforced by a template test. ALWAYS/NEVER reserved for true invariants (attribution, destructive ops, TDD ordering); judgment calls get decision rules.

AGENTS.md sections (template `templates/AGENTS.md.tpl`, `{{TEST_CMD}}`-style variables filled by `baton init` from detection): 1 Project (one paragraph + directory map) · 2 Roles and models (points at committed config; "resolve via `baton remap`, never hardcode model names") · 3 Working phases (research → plan → test-authoring → implementation → review → handoff, one-line entry/exit criteria, "state your phase") · 4 TDD contract (no implementation before failing tests exist and are verifier-approved; test changes re-enter verification; progress claims must cite command output) · 5 Review gates (Gate 1 plan, per-subtask test review, Gate 2 dual independent final review; verdicts APPROVED/APPROVED_WITH_NOTES/BLOCKED) · 6 Handoff protocol (when/how to checkpoint and resume) · 7 Boundaries and stop rules (never force-push/hard-reset/delete without explicit instruction; pause for scope change/irreversible/credentials; otherwise "when you have enough information to act, act") · 8 Validation (command table; done = green + gate verdict) · 9 Git and attribution (sole author SakshamUboweja; zero AI attribution invariant; **commit cadence rule: commit after every completed subtask/green TDD cycle — small, regular commits to main so contributions accrue to Saksham's GitHub profile**; never commit `.handoff/`) · 10 Memory (`.handoff/notes.md`, gitignored, rides the bundle across handoffs).

CLAUDE.md shim (~15 lines): line 1 `@AGENTS.md`; then ≤8 Claude-specific bullets — subagent delegation for parallel research/verification, concrete per-role mechanics (reviewer roles via codex companion, test-author via Agent-tool model override), plans in `docs/plans/`, attribution-setting reminder, **commit-cadence reminder (commit each completed subtask; never batch a day's work into one commit)**, prefer `/baton:*` commands, **user-facing outputs simple and concise: outcome first, plain language, no jargon walls**. Nothing duplicating AGENTS.md; no reasoning-echo instructions.

**One-time machine setup (first implementation act, needs your approval since it touches global files)**: append to `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md` — (a) "present answers and outputs simply and concisely: outcome first, short sentences, no unnecessary detail"; (b) "when working in repos, commit completed work regularly and authored solely as SakshamUboweja"; set `{"attribution": {"commit": "", "pr": ""}}` in `~/.claude/settings.json`; save these as persistent memory.

The baton repo dogfoods both from commit one, with a CI check (`baton init --check`) asserting rendered files match template output.

     1	import { hostname } from 'node:os';
     2	
     3	// Best-effort extraction of baton events from raw harness hook payloads.
     4	// Total: any input value yields an Event[] — garbage degrades to note events,
     5	// never a throw, so a checkpoint can never break the host harness.
     6	
     7	/** @typedef {{host: string, pid: number, startTime: number | null}} SessionFacts */
     8	
     9	/** @type {Record<string, string>} */
    10	const FILE_TOOL_OPS = { Write: 'write', Edit: 'edit' };
    11	
    12	/** @returns {SessionFacts} */
    13	function processFacts() {
    14	  return { host: hostname(), pid: process.pid, startTime: Math.round(performance.timeOrigin) };
    15	}
    16	
    17	/**
    18	 * A process-scoped hint for payloads lacking a stable session id. Deterministic
    19	 * over the identity facts: one process => one hint; unstable hints never
    20	 * trigger foreign-session rejection on their own (enforced at the command).
    21	 * @param {SessionFacts | undefined} session
    22	 */
    23	function generatedHint(session) {
    24	  const f = session ?? processFacts();
    25	  return ['unstable', f.host, f.pid, f.startTime].join(':');
    26	}
    27	
    28	/**
    29	 * @param {any} raw
    30	 * @param {SessionFacts | undefined} session
    31	 * @returns {{sessionHint: string, unstable: boolean}}
    32	 */
    33	function deriveHint(raw, session) {
    34	  const id = raw && typeof raw === 'object' ? raw.session_id : undefined;
    35	  if (typeof id === 'string' && id.length > 0) return { sessionHint: id, unstable: false };
    36	  return { sessionHint: generatedHint(session), unstable: true };
    37	}
    38	
    39	/**
    40	 * Normalize a parsed hook payload into baton/event@1 events. Adapters that
    41	 * already speak baton/event@1 pass through verbatim; raw per-harness payloads
    42	 * get best-effort extraction; unknowns degrade to note events. Never throws.
    43	 * @param {any} raw parsed JS value (the caller owns JSON.parse)
    44	 * @param {string} platform 'claude-code' | 'codex' | 'cursor'
    45	 * @param {SessionFacts} [session] identity facts for generated hints; defaults to this process
    46	 * @returns {any[]}
    47	 */
    48	export function normalizeHookPayload(raw, platform, session) {
    49	  // Passthrough tier: the adapter formed the events; return them untouched.
    50	  if (raw && typeof raw === 'object' && raw.schema === 'baton/event@1') {
    51	    if (Array.isArray(raw.events)) return raw.events;
    52	    return [raw];
    53	  }
    54	
    55	  const { sessionHint, unstable } = deriveHint(raw, session);
    56	  /** @param {string} type @param {Record<string, any>} payload */
    57	  const event = (type, payload) => ({ type, payload, source: platform, sessionHint, unstable });
    58	  /** @param {string} trigger */
    59	  const note = (trigger) => event('note', { trigger, text: 'hook ' + trigger + ' observed on ' + platform });
    60	
    61	  const eventName =
    62	    raw && typeof raw === 'object'
    63	      ? (typeof raw.hook_event_name === 'string' && raw.hook_event_name) ||
    64	        (platform === 'cursor' && typeof raw.event === 'string' && raw.event) ||
    65	        null
    66	      : null;
    67	  if (!eventName) return [note('unknown')];
    68	
    69	  if (platform === 'claude-code') {
    70	    if (eventName === 'PostToolUse') {
    71	      const op = FILE_TOOL_OPS[raw.tool_name];
    72	      const path = raw.tool_input && typeof raw.tool_input === 'object' ? raw.tool_input.file_path : undefined;
    73	      if (op && typeof path === 'string') return [event('file.touch', { path, op })];
    74	      return [note('PostToolUse')];
    75	    }
    76	    if (eventName === 'StopFailure') {
    77	      const errorType = raw.error && typeof raw.error === 'object' && typeof raw.error.type === 'string' ? raw.error.type : 'unknown';
    78	      return [
    79	        event('note', {
    80	          trigger: 'StopFailure',
    81	          text: 'turn ended on API error: ' + errorType,
    82	          errorType,
    83	          structured: { kind: 'stop-failure', errorType },
    84	        }),
    85	      ];
    86	    }
    87	    return [note(eventName)];
    88	  }
    89	
    90	  // Codex and Cursor (and any future platform string): every lifecycle event
    91	  // is a note carrying its trigger; the harness is the source.
    92	  return [note(eventName)];
    93	}

/bin/zsh -lc "nl -ba core/src/roles/resolve.mjs | sed -n '1,280p'; nl -ba tests/unit/resolve-eligibility.test.mjs | sed -n '1,320p'; rg -n -C 6 \"outcome.*error|outcome.*timeout|degraded\" tests/unit/resolve*.test.mjs" in /Users/saksham/baton
 succeeded in 0ms:
     1	/**
     2	 * Pure, explainable role resolution over the committed matrix. Every decision
     3	 * is auditable: chosen entries carry their chainIndex; passed-over entries land
     4	 * in skipped[] with a why. avoid[] population is the caller's job.
     5	 * @param {{config: any, to: string, avoid?: string[], nativeOnly?: boolean, probes?: Record<string, {capability: string, outcome: string}> | null}} input
     6	 * @returns {{assignments: Record<string, any>, notes: string[]}}
     7	 */
     8	export function resolveRoles({ config, to, avoid = [], nativeOnly = false, probes = null }) {
     9	  /** @type {string[]} */
    10	  const notes = [];
    11	
    12	  /**
    13	   * The eligibility verdict for a platform, or null when eligible.
    14	   * @param {string} platform @returns {string | null}
    15	   */
    16	  function skipReason(platform) {
    17	    if (avoid.includes(platform)) return 'avoided';
    18	    if (!(platform in (config.platforms ?? {}))) return 'unknown-platform';
    19	    if (config.platforms[platform]?.enabled === false) return 'disabled';
    20	    if (probes && probes[platform]) {
    21	      const { capability, outcome } = probes[platform];
    22	      if (outcome === 'rate-limited') return 'rate-limited';
    23	      if (capability === 'not-installed') return 'not-installed';
    24	      // 'installed' means installation was PROVEN but the probe was capped there
    25	      // (capOnSuccess) — authentication is UNVERIFIED, not verified-failed. Only
    26	      // a probe that ran and did NOT succeed (outcome !== 'ok') is a verified
    27	      // auth/reachability failure worth skipping; a capped success stays
    28	      // selectable and is flagged degraded below (iter-3 F1, plan §Role matrix).
    29	      if (capability === 'installed' && outcome !== 'ok') return 'unauthenticated';
    30	    }
    31	    return null;
    32	  }
    33	
    34	  // A selection is degraded when its availability is UNVERIFIED (gate-2 iter-2
    35	  // M3, iter-3 F1): with no probe cache at all, resolution is offline and EVERY
    36	  // selection is degraded (plan: "Offline resolution always succeeds, flagged
    37	  // degraded"); with a partial cache, platforms the cache is missing are; and a
    38	  // platform probed only to 'installed' (auth unverified, e.g. a --version
    39	  // success) is degraded even though it stays selectable.
    40	  /** @param {string} platform */
    41	  const probeUnverified = (platform) => {
    42	    if (probes == null || !probes[platform]) return true;
    43	    const { capability, outcome } = probes[platform];
    44	    // Degraded when ANY required dimension is unverified: a capped 'installed'
    45	    // (auth never confirmed) OR a non-ok outcome (iter-4 I6) — a selectable
    46	    // platform whose last probe ERRORED or TIMED OUT is presented as
    47	    // reachability-verified otherwise, which is exactly the silent-degradation
    48	    // the plan's degraded flag exists to surface. (rate-limited is skipped, not
    49	    // selected, so it never reaches here.)
    50	    return capability === 'installed' || outcome !== 'ok';
    51	  };
    52	
    53	  /** @type {Record<string, any>} */
    54	  const assignments = {};
    55	  for (const [role, chain] of Object.entries(config.roles)) {
    56	    /** @type {any[]} */
    57	    const skipped = [];
    58	    /** @type {any} */
    59	    let assignment = null;
    60	
    61	    for (let i = 0; i < chain.length; i += 1) {
    62	      const entry = chain[i];
    63	      if (nativeOnly && entry.platform !== to) {
    64	        skipped.push({ chainIndex: i, platform: entry.platform, model: entry.model, why: 'non-native' });
    65	        continue;
    66	      }
    67	      const why = skipReason(entry.platform);
    68	      if (why) {
    69	        skipped.push({ chainIndex: i, platform: entry.platform, model: entry.model, why });
    70	        continue;
    71	      }
    72	      assignment = {
    73	        platform: entry.platform,
    74	        model: entry.model,
    75	        effort: entry.effort ?? null,
    76	        mode: entry.platform === to ? 'native' : 'delegated',
    77	        chainIndex: i,
    78	        skipped,
    79	      };
    80	      if (probeUnverified(entry.platform)) assignment.degraded = true;
    81	      break;
    82	    }
    83	
    84	    if (!assignment && nativeOnly) {
    85	      // Forced-default is only valid when the destination itself is eligible.
    86	      const whyTo = skipReason(to);
    87	      const model = config.defaults?.[to];
    88	      if (!whyTo && typeof model === 'string') {
    89	        assignment = { platform: to, model, effort: null, mode: 'forced-default', chainIndex: null, skipped };
    90	        if (probeUnverified(to)) assignment.degraded = true;
    91	      }
    92	    }
    93	
    94	    if (!assignment) {
    95	      assignment = { platform: null, model: null, effort: null, mode: 'unavailable', chainIndex: null, skipped };
    96	    }
    97	    assignments[role] = assignment;
    98	  }
    99	
   100	  return { assignments, notes };
   101	}

     2	import assert from 'node:assert/strict';
     3	import { resolveRoles } from '../../core/src/roles/resolve.mjs';
     4	
     5	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
     6	// Gate-2 iteration-3 F1 (reviewer-a #6, reviewer-b #1): doctor caps a healthy
     7	// `claude --version` probe at capability 'installed' (capOnSuccess), so the
     8	// probe cache doctor writes carries {capability:'installed', outcome:'ok'} for a
     9	// fully healthy claude-code. A version probe leaves AUTH UNVERIFIED — not
    10	// verified-failed — so the plan (§Role matrix) requires selectable-with-degraded,
    11	// NOT skipped-as-unauthenticated. The resolver must only skip 'unauthenticated'
    12	// on a VERIFIED auth failure (installed AND outcome !== 'ok').
    13	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    14	
    15	const CONFIG = {
    16	  schema: 'baton/config@1',
    17	  roles: {
    18	    ccFirst: [{ platform: 'claude-code', model: 'claude-fable-5' }, { platform: 'codex', model: 'gpt-5.6-sol' }],
    19	    ccOnly: [{ platform: 'claude-code', model: 'claude-fable-5' }],
    20	  },
    21	  platforms: { 'claude-code': {}, codex: {}, cursor: {} },
    22	  defaults: { 'claude-code': 'claude-fable-5', codex: 'gpt-5.6-sol', cursor: 'composer' },
    23	};
    24	
    25	// The exact cache shape doctor writes on a healthy machine: claude-code capped
    26	// at 'installed' by capOnSuccess, codex/cursor probed to 'reachable'.
    27	const HEALTHY_CACHE = {
    28	  'claude-code': { capability: 'installed', outcome: 'ok' },
    29	  codex: { capability: 'reachable', outcome: 'ok' },
    30	  cursor: { capability: 'reachable', outcome: 'ok' },
    31	};
    32	
    33	describe('F1 — a healthy version-probed platform is selectable-but-degraded, not skipped', () => {
    34	  it('claude-code {installed, ok} is SELECTED (degraded) where first-eligible, not skipped as unauthenticated', () => {
    35	    const { assignments } = resolveRoles({ config: CONFIG, to: 'codex', probes: HEALTHY_CACHE });
    36	    const cc = assignments.ccFirst;
    37	    assert.equal(cc.platform, 'claude-code', 'a version-probed claude-code stays selectable at chain head');
    38	    assert.equal(cc.degraded, true, 'auth-unverified selection carries the degraded flag');
    39	    assert.ok(!(cc.skipped ?? []).some((/** @type {any} */ s) => s.platform === 'claude-code'), 'claude-code is not skipped');
    40	  });
    41	
    42	  it('a claude-code-only role resolves to claude-code (degraded), never "unavailable"', () => {
    43	    const { assignments } = resolveRoles({ config: CONFIG, to: 'claude-code', probes: HEALTHY_CACHE });
    44	    assert.equal(assignments.ccOnly.mode, 'native');
    45	    assert.equal(assignments.ccOnly.platform, 'claude-code');
    46	    assert.equal(assignments.ccOnly.degraded, true);
    47	  });
    48	
    49	  it('a VERIFIED auth/reachability failure ({installed, error}) IS skipped as unauthenticated', () => {
    50	    const probes = { ...HEALTHY_CACHE, 'claude-code': { capability: 'installed', outcome: 'error' } };
    51	    const { assignments } = resolveRoles({ config: CONFIG, to: 'codex', probes });
    52	    const cc = assignments.ccFirst;
    53	    assert.equal(cc.platform, 'codex', 'a verified-failed claude-code falls through to codex');
    54	    assert.ok((cc.skipped ?? []).some((/** @type {any} */ s) => s.platform === 'claude-code' && s.why === 'unauthenticated'), 'recorded as unauthenticated');
    55	  });
    56	
    57	  it('a non-ok OUTCOME on a selected platform flags degraded (iter-4 I6)', () => {
    58	    // capability is fine but the last probe ERRORED/TIMED OUT — the selection is
    59	    // still made (capability ≥ authenticated, outcome ≠ rate-limited) but must be
    60	    // flagged degraded, not presented as verified-healthy.
    61	    const errored = { ...HEALTHY_CACHE, codex: { capability: 'authenticated', outcome: 'error' } };
    62	    const codexPick = resolveRoles({ config: { ...CONFIG, roles: { r: [{ platform: 'codex', model: 'gpt-5.6-sol' }] } }, to: 'codex', probes: errored }).assignments.r;
    63	    assert.equal(codexPick.platform, 'codex', 'still selectable');
    64	    assert.equal(codexPick.degraded, true, 'a non-ok outcome degrades the selection');
    65	
    66	    const timedOut = { ...HEALTHY_CACHE, codex: { capability: 'reachable', outcome: 'timeout' } };
    67	    const codexPick2 = resolveRoles({ config: { ...CONFIG, roles: { r: [{ platform: 'codex', model: 'gpt-5.6-sol' }] } }, to: 'codex', probes: timedOut }).assignments.r;
    68	    assert.equal(codexPick2.degraded, true, 'a timeout outcome degrades the selection');
    69	  });
    70	
    71	  it('a fully-reachable platform still resolves without a degraded flag', () => {
    72	    const { assignments } = resolveRoles({ config: CONFIG, to: 'codex', probes: HEALTHY_CACHE });
    73	    // codex is reachable+ok → the delegated pick for a codex-native role is not degraded.
    74	    const codexPick = resolveRoles({ config: { ...CONFIG, roles: { r: [{ platform: 'codex', model: 'gpt-5.6-sol' }] } }, to: 'codex', probes: HEALTHY_CACHE }).assignments.r;
    75	    assert.equal(codexPick.degraded ?? false, false, 'a reachable+ok platform is not degraded');

     1	// Path-spelling normalization shared by every containment/ascent check (plan
     2	// §Root discovery, §Threat model, Windows + packaging). Windows realpathSync
     3	// returns backslash paths and Node tolerates forward slashes, so all comparisons
     4	// happen in one canonical forward-slash space: backslashes → slashes, collapsed
     5	// duplicate separators, and no trailing separator (except a bare root). Doing
     6	// this in one place stopped the recurring "fixed jail, forgot resolveRoot /
     7	// transcript containment" class of bugs.
     8	
     9	/**
    10	 * Canonical forward-slash spelling: `\`→`/`, `//`→`/`, trailing `/` trimmed
    11	 * (a lone `/` is preserved).
    12	 * @param {any} p
    13	 * @returns {string}
    14	 */
    15	export function normSep(p) {
    16	  let s = String(p).replace(/\\/g, '/').replace(/\/+/g, '/');
    17	  if (s.length > 1 && s.endsWith('/')) s = s.slice(0, -1);
    18	  return s;
    19	}
    20	
    21	/**
    22	 * Join a base and a single child segment without producing a doubled separator
    23	 * (so `C:/` + `.handoff` → `C:/.handoff`, not `C://.handoff`).
    24	 * @param {string} base @param {string} child
    25	 * @returns {string}
    26	 */
    27	export function joinNorm(base, child) {
    28	  return normSep(`${base}/${child}`);
    29	}
    30	
    31	/**
    32	 * True when `child` IS `base` or is contained under it, compared on normalized
    33	 * forward-slash spellings (volume-aware; `C:/repo` contains `C:/repo/.handoff`
    34	 * but not `C:/repository`).
    35	 * @param {string} base @param {string} child
    36	 * @returns {boolean}
    37	 */
    38	export function isContained(base, child) {
    39	  const b = normSep(base);
    40	  const c = normSep(child);
    41	  // A root base already ends in the separator ('/'), so appending another would
    42	  // form '//' and reject every child (iter-4 I8: isContained('/', '/x') was
    43	  // false). Use the base itself as the prefix when it already ends in '/'.
    44	  const prefix = b.endsWith('/') ? b : `${b}/`;
    45	  return c === b || c.startsWith(prefix);
    46	}

 succeeded in 0ms:
     1	// Threat-model guards (plan §Threat model): bundle-borne paths and the managed
     2	// .handoff tree are untrusted input. jailRelPath is the single pure gate every
     3	// repository path passes through before storage or render; checkHandoffTree
     4	// refuses to operate on a managed tree that contains symlinks or resolves
     5	// outside the repository root.
     6	
     7	import { normSep, joinNorm } from './pathnorm.mjs';
     8	
     9	const NUL = String.fromCharCode(0);
    10	
    11	/**
    12	 * Normalize an untrusted repository-relative path, or return null when it is
    13	 * unsafe: non-string, empty, NUL bytes, absolute (posix, Windows drive, UNC),
    14	 * or escaping the root via `..`. Backslashes are treated as separators so a
    15	 * Windows spelling cannot smuggle a traversal past a posix check.
    16	 * @param {any} p
    17	 * @returns {string | null}
    18	 */
    19	export function jailRelPath(p) {
    20	  if (typeof p !== 'string' || p.length === 0 || p.includes(NUL)) return null;
    21	  const unified = p.replace(/\\/g, '/');
    22	  if (unified.startsWith('/')) return null;
    23	  if (/^[A-Za-z]:/.test(unified)) return null;
    24	  /** @type {string[]} */
    25	  const out = [];
    26	  for (const seg of unified.split('/')) {
    27	    if (seg === '' || seg === '.') continue;
    28	    if (seg === '..') {
    29	      if (out.length === 0) return null;
    30	      out.pop();
    31	      continue;
    32	    }
    33	    out.push(seg);
    34	  }
    35	  if (out.length === 0) return null;
    36	  return out.join('/');
    37	}
    38	
    39	/** Thrown by store mutations when the managed tree fails the jail check. */
    40	export class UnsafeTreeError extends Error {}
    41	
    42	/**
    43	 * Verify the managed .handoff tree is safe to operate on: the directory itself
    44	 * is not a symlink, its realpath stays under the repository root, and no entry
    45	 * anywhere in the tree is a symlink (lstat walk — never follows links). An
    46	 * absent tree is safe; an unverifiable tree is refused, never trusted.
    47	 * @param {string} root @param {any} io
    48	 * @returns {{ok: true} | {ok: false, problem: string}}
    49	 */
    50	export function checkHandoffTree(root, io) {
    51	  const dir = `${root}/.handoff`;
    52	  try {
    53	    if (!io.fs.existsSync(dir)) return { ok: true };
    54	    if (io.fs.lstatSync(dir).isSymbolicLink()) {
    55	      return { ok: false, problem: `.handoff is a symlink — refusing to operate through a linked managed tree` };
    56	    }
    57	    // Compare on a canonical forward-slash spelling (Windows: realpathSync
    58	    // returns backslash paths; a drive-root repo would also yield a doubled
    59	    // `C://.handoff`). joinNorm collapses both so containment is platform-neutral
    60	    // and drive-root-safe (iter-3 F6).
    61	    const expected = joinNorm(io.fs.realpathSync(root), '.handoff');
    62	    const dirReal = normSep(io.fs.realpathSync(dir));
    63	    if (dirReal !== expected) {
    64	      return { ok: false, problem: `.handoff resolves outside the repository root (${dirReal}) — refusing` };
    65	    }
    66	    // Walk is race-tolerant (gate-2 iter-2): under concurrent atomic writes
    67	    // the tree churns — tmp files, the lock dir, and rotated journals appear
    68	    // and vanish between readdir and lstat. An entry that disappears mid-walk
    69	    // (ENOENT) is normal concurrency, NOT a symlink attack, so it is skipped;
    70	    // only a POSITIVELY observed symlink refuses. The stable top-level checks
    71	    // above (.handoff itself a symlink / realpath escape) stay strict.
    72	    /** @type {string[]} */
    73	    const stack = [dir];
    74	    while (stack.length > 0) {
    75	      const d = /** @type {string} */ (stack.pop());
    76	      /** @type {string[]} */
    77	      let names;
    78	      try {
    79	        names = io.fs.readdirSync(d);
    80	      } catch (e) {
    81	        if (/** @type {any} */ (e)?.code === 'ENOENT') continue; // subdir removed mid-walk
    82	        throw e;
    83	      }
    84	      for (const name of names) {
    85	        const p = `${d}/${name}`;
    86	        let st;
    87	        try {
    88	          st = io.fs.lstatSync(p);
    89	        } catch (e) {
    90	          if (/** @type {any} */ (e)?.code === 'ENOENT') continue; // entry vanished mid-walk
    91	          throw e;
    92	        }
    93	        if (st.isSymbolicLink()) return { ok: false, problem: `${p} is a symlink — refusing (managed-tree jail)` };
    94	        if (st.isDirectory()) stack.push(p);
    95	      }
    96	    }
    97	    return { ok: true };
    98	  } catch (err) {
    99	    const msg = /** @type {any} */ (err)?.message ?? String(err);
   100	    return { ok: false, problem: `managed tree could not be verified (${msg}) — refusing` };
   101	  }
   102	}
   103	
   104	/**
   105	 * Assert form of checkHandoffTree for mutation paths: throws UnsafeTreeError
   106	 * so commands surface the refusal through their existing soft-failure handling.
   107	 * @param {string} root @param {any} io
   108	 */
   109	export function assertHandoffTreeSafe(root, io) {
   110	  const r = checkHandoffTree(root, io);
   111	  if (!r.ok) throw new UnsafeTreeError(r.problem);
   112	}
     1	import { normSep, joinNorm } from '../util/pathnorm.mjs';
     2	
     3	/**
     4	 * The --json contract: exactly one compact envelope line on stdout, nothing else.
     5	 * @param {any} io
     6	 * @param {{ok: boolean, data?: any, warnings?: any[], error?: {code: string, msg: string} | null}} env
     7	 */
     8	export function emitEnvelope(io, env) {
     9	  const full = { ok: env.ok, data: env.data ?? null, warnings: env.warnings ?? [], error: env.error ?? null };
    10	  io.stdout.write(JSON.stringify(full) + '\n');
    11	}
    12	
    13	/**
    14	 * Centralized usage-error emission (gate-2 fix 11): with --json the envelope
    15	 * contract holds even for bad invocations; otherwise a one-line stderr
    16	 * message. Always returns the usage exit code 2.
    17	 * @param {any} io @param {Record<string, string | boolean>} flags
    18	 * @param {string} cmd @param {string} msg
    19	 * @returns {number}
    20	 */
    21	export function usageError(io, flags, cmd, msg) {
    22	  if (flags.json === true) emitEnvelope(io, { ok: false, error: { code: 'usage', msg } });
    23	  else io.stderr.write(`baton ${cmd}: ${msg}\n`);
    24	  return 2;
    25	}
    26	
    27	/**
    28	 * Repo-root discovery (plan §Root discovery): `--root` wins; else the nearest
    29	 * ancestor of io.cwd carrying `.handoff/` or `baton.config.json`; else the
    30	 * nearest ancestor carrying `.git` (the repo toplevel — and a boundary the
    31	 * walk never crosses, so a nested repo resolves to itself); else io.cwd.
    32	 * @param {any} io @param {Record<string, string | boolean>} flags
    33	 * @returns {string}
    34	 */
    35	export function resolveRoot(io, flags) {
    36	  if (typeof flags.root === 'string' && flags.root.length > 0) return flags.root;
    37	  // Normalize separators up front (gate-2 iter-2 M2): a Windows cwd arrives
    38	  // backslash-separated, but Node fs accepts forward slashes, so the ascent
    39	  // and the marker checks work in one forward-slash space. The last separator
    40	  // is either kind; the loop stops at a drive/UNC or filesystem root.
    41	  const start = normSep(io.cwd);
    42	  let dir = start;
    43	  while (true) {
    44	    try {
    45	      if (io.fs.existsSync(joinNorm(dir, '.handoff')) || io.fs.existsSync(joinNorm(dir, 'baton.config.json'))) return dir;
    46	      if (io.fs.existsSync(joinNorm(dir, '.git'))) return dir; // repo toplevel; never walk past a .git boundary
    47	    } catch {
    48	      break;
    49	    }
    50	    const cut = dir.lastIndexOf('/');
    51	    if (cut < 0) break;
    52	    const parent = cut === 0 ? '/' : dir.slice(0, cut);
    53	    // At a drive-relative ceiling ("C:") examine the drive ROOT ("C:/") once —
    54	    // a repo can live directly at C:/ (iter-3 F6) — then stop.
    55	    if (/^[A-Za-z]:$/.test(parent)) {
    56	      const driveRoot = `${parent}/`;
    57	      if (driveRoot !== dir) {
    58	        dir = driveRoot;
    59	        continue;
    60	      }
    61	      break;
    62	    }
    63	    if (parent === dir) break;
    64	    dir = parent;
    65	  }
    66	  return start;
    67	}
    68	
    69	/**
    70	 * Minimal flag parser: `--key value` pairs (value = next token not starting
    71	 * with --), bare `--key` booleans, positionals collected in order.
    72	 * @param {string[]} args
    73	 * @returns {{flags: Record<string, string | boolean>, positionals: string[]}}
    74	 */
    75	export function parseFlags(args) {
    76	  /** @type {Record<string, string | boolean>} */
    77	  const flags = {};
    78	  /** @type {string[]} */
    79	  const positionals = [];
    80	  for (let i = 0; i < args.length; i += 1) {
    81	    const a = args[i];
    82	    if (a.startsWith('--')) {
    83	      const key = a.slice(2);
    84	      const next = args[i + 1];
    85	      if (next !== undefined && !next.startsWith('--')) {
    86	        flags[key] = next;
    87	        i += 1;
    88	      } else {
    89	        flags[key] = true;
    90	      }
    91	    } else {
    92	      positionals.push(a);
    93	    }
    94	  }
    95	  return { flags, positionals };
    96	}

    13	export function bundlePaths(root) {
    14	  const dir = `${root}/.handoff`;
    15	  return {
    16	    dir,
    17	    snapshot: `${dir}/bundle.json`,
    18	    bak: `${dir}/bundle.json.bak`,
